Browse All / Statin Toxicity

Statin Toxicity

Complex MONDO:0033170 Pathograph 36 Show in embeddings browser Poisoning

Statin toxicity is the adverse-effect spectrum of HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor therapy. One drug-target engagement produces it, but the engagement is shared with the drug's intended action, so the entry is organised by what the drug does where it is not wanted rather than by a lesion the patient carries. Statins inhibit HMG-CoA reductase in skeletal muscle as well as in liver, and the resulting loss of mevalonate-pathway isoprenoid intermediates - not the loss of cholesterol itself - is what experimental rescue points to as the proximate cause of myocyte injury. Above that molecular step the entry splits into strata that share almost nothing clinically: reported muscle symptoms with a normal creatine kinase, creatine-kinase-defined myopathy, rhabdomyolysis with pigment nephropathy, an uncommon anti-HMGCR immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy that persists after the drug is stopped, aminotransferase elevation, and an excess of new-onset diabetes put at around 10% relative risk by randomised trials. The strata carry different denominators, different diagnostic routes and opposite management - a careful rechallenge is appropriate for ordinary muscle symptoms and is permanently avoided once anti-HMGCR disease is confirmed - so they are kept apart as subtypes rather than merged into a single frequency. Host susceptibility is pharmacokinetic, not Mendelian: reduced-function alleles of the hepatic uptake transporter SLCO1B1 and the efflux transporter ABCG2 raise systemic drug exposure, which raises risk without being necessary or sufficient for injury.

Subtypes

5
Statin-associated muscle symptoms (CK normal or minimally raised)
Symmetrical muscle aching, cramp, stiffness or subjective weakness arising after initiation or dose escalation, with creatine kinase normal or only mildly raised and no objective weakness. Improves on dechallenge. Distinguished from the strata below by the absence of biochemical muscle injury, and managed by switching statin, lowering the dose, alternate-day dosing or combination lipid-lowering therapy rather than by stopping lipid-lowering treatment altogether. Immunosuppression has no role.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35969116 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"However, complete statin intolerance occurs in only a small minority of treated patients (estimated prevalence of only 3-5%)."
Separates inability to tolerate any statin from the much more commonly reported symptom syndrome, which is the distinction this subtype exists to hold.
PMID:25694464 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Statin-associated muscle symptoms cover a broader range of clinical presentations, usually with normal or minimally elevated CK levels, with a prevalence of 7-29% in registries and observational studies."
The consensus definition of this stratum, including the normal or minimally raised creatine kinase that distinguishes it from the strata below. Its 7-29% figure is reported prevalence in registries and observational studies, not causally attributable toxicity, which is why no frequency slot is filled from it.
Statin-induced myopathy and rhabdomyolysis
Creatine-kinase-defined muscle injury, graded in the clinical literature by multiples of the upper limit of normal, extending to rhabdomyolysis with myoglobin release and acute kidney injury. Dose- and exposure-dependent, and the stratum in which the SEARCH trial's simvastatin dose comparison measured a thirty-fold difference in incidence. Requires immediate drug withdrawal and assessment of renal function, rather than rechallenge.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"CK levels in severe myopathy and rhabdomyolysis (SRM 4 and 5, respectively) are raised above 10 times and 50 times the ULN, respectively"
Gives the biochemical grading that separates this stratum from SAMS, where creatine kinase is normal or minimally raised.
Statin-triggered anti-HMGCR immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy
An uncommon autoimmune myopathy in which statin exposure is followed by an antibody response against HMG-CoA reductase itself. Progressive symmetrical proximal weakness with markedly raised creatine kinase that persists or worsens after the statin is stopped, and biopsy showing myofiber necrosis and regeneration with comparatively sparse lymphocytic infiltrate. Diagnosis rests on anti-HMGCR serology; treatment is specialist-directed immunosuppression, and statin re-exposure is permanently avoided. This is the one stratum where drug withdrawal is not sufficient treatment.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41010829 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The initial creatine kinase (CK) levels ranged from 1438 to over 13,000 IU/L."
Documents the markedly raised creatine kinase that characterises this stratum, far above the normal or minimally raised values that define SAMS.
Statin-associated aminotransferase elevation and liver injury
Usually asymptomatic alanine and aspartate aminotransferase elevation; clinically important drug-induced liver injury is uncommon. A trial safety threshold of three times the upper limit of normal is used for reassessment, but a mild isolated elevation is neither proof of hepatocellular destruction nor necessarily of hepatic origin, since aspartate aminotransferase also rises from injured muscle.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38751493 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The deteriorated liver function, especially the increase of serum alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and/or aspartate aminotransferase (AST), is one of the most common side effects of statins."
Establishes aminotransferase elevation as a distinct and common manifestation, separate from the muscle strata.
Statin-associated dysglycemia and new-onset diabetes
New-onset diabetes in excess during treatment, put at around 10% relative risk by randomised trials, greater at higher treatment intensity and concentrated in people with baseline metabolic risk. Detected by fasting glucose or glycated hemoglobin rather than by creatine kinase, and managed by metabolic treatment and benefit-risk reassessment rather than by automatic statin cessation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39169014 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"High-intensity statin was a significant predictor of NODM after adjusting for other co-variables"
Supports treatment intensity as a determinant of this stratum, which is what distinguishes it from the dose-independent attribution problem in SAMS.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
What proportion of muscle symptoms reported during statin treatment is caused by the drug, and can any individual case be attributed without a blinded rechallenge?
CONTROVERSY OPEN attribution_of_muscle_symptoms_to_the_drug
Attached to
phenotypes#Myalgia has_subtypes#SAMS
This is the entry's central difficulty and it is deliberately not drawn as pathophysiology, because the competing explanations are not mechanisms of the drug: exercise, an untreated thyroid or musculoskeletal disorder, intercurrent illness, and expectation. Reported symptom prevalence in registries runs far above what blinded comparison supports, and the International Lipid Expert Panel puts the expectation effect at possibly over half of all reported muscle weakness or pain. The consequence for this entry is structural: the frequency of reported symptoms and the frequency of drug-caused injury are different quantities with different denominators, so no frequency slot is filled from a reported-symptom rate anywhere in the SAMS stratum. It is recorded as a controversy rather than a knowledge gap because the evidence exists and is contested in its interpretation, not absent.
This is why exercise is named as a confounder in the creatine kinase and myalgia descriptions rather than linked as an environmental exposure: in this entry exercise is an alternative explanation for the observation, not a modifier of the drug's mechanism.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35969116 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"subjective symptoms occur as a result of the fact that patients expect them to do so when taking medicines (the nocebo/drucebo effect)-what might be truth even for over 50% of all patients with muscle weakness/pain"
States the expectation effect and the scale the panel attributes to it, which is the reason reported symptom rates cannot be read as rates of drug-caused injury.
PMID:38384718 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Observational studies are valuable; however, the lack of a placebo comparator limits the ability to establish a causal relationship between statins and muscle complaints with exercise."
Names the design limitation that makes attribution the open question, in the specific setting - exercise - that is the commonest competing explanation for muscle symptoms in a treated population.
Does depletion of ubiquinone explain statin myopathy, and is there any role for coenzyme Q10 supplementation?
CONTROVERSY OPEN ubiquinone_depletion_as_the_myopathy_mechanism
Attached to
pathophysiology#Depletion of Mevalonate Pathway Isoprenoid Intermediates pathophysiology#Myocyte Bioenergetic Failure
Ubiquinone is a mevalonate-pathway product, statins deplete it, and it is required for mitochondrial electron transport - which makes its depletion the oldest and most intuitive candidate mechanism for the muscle injury, and the rationale for coenzyme Q10 supplementation. The rescue experiment argues against it: adding coenzyme Q10 back to statin-exposed myotubes does not prevent the fall in cellular energy, while adding the geranylgeranyl donor does. That is why this entry draws its edge through geranylgeranylation and draws no edge through ubiquinone, and why no coenzyme Q10 treatment is recorded. The question stays open rather than closed because a single in vitro rescue in a mouse cell line does not settle a clinical question.
Reduced ubiquinone and reduced mitochondrial content have been reported in statin-exposed human muscle by work not cited in this entry. Those are observations of depletion; this discussion is about whether the depletion carries the injury, which is a different question and the one the rescue experiment addresses.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Simultaneous addition of cholesterol or CoQ was unable to prevent the simvastatin-mediated decrease in ATP levels (Figure 1, panel b)."
The negative rescue result. Replacing ubiquinone does not prevent the bioenergetic injury, which is the evidence against ubiquinone depletion being the operative step and the reason no such edge is drawn.
Does terminal complement activation carry the myofiber injury in anti-HMGCR immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy, or is the complement deposition seen on biopsy a consequence of injury rather than its cause?
CONTROVERSY OPEN complement_effector_in_anti_hmgcr_imnm
Attached to
pathophysiology#Anti-HMGCR Autoantibody Response pathophysiology#Autoimmune Myofiber Necrosis and Regeneration
Complement deposition on myofibers is part of the histological picture, and passive transfer of anti-HMGCR antibodies to mice has been reported to cause weakness and necrosis in a complement-dependent setting, which together motivated terminal complement inhibition as a therapy. A randomised phase 2 trial of the C5 inhibitor zilucoplan then showed no benefit despite target engagement. That does not establish that complement is uninvolved in the biology, but it does mean no complement-mediated effector edge can be drawn in this pathograph: the one intervention that tested the step directly in patients was negative. The question matters because the effector mechanism is what a targeted treatment for this stratum would have to act on, and immunosuppression is currently non-specific.
The trial enrolled 27 patients with anti-HMGCR or anti-SRP antibodies, so it is small and not confined to the statin-associated subset, and it is registered as terminated (see clinical_trials NCT04025632). Read as evidence against C5 inhibition as a treatment, and as a reason not to assert a complement effector edge, rather than as a demonstration that complement plays no part.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36923454 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"At Week 8 there were no clinically relevant or statistically significant differences, despite target engagement based on mode of action, between treatment arms in mean percent change (standard deviation) of CK levels versus baseline"
The negative randomised result that leaves the effector step open. Quoted for the absence of difference despite confirmed target engagement, which is what makes it informative about the mechanism rather than only about the drug.
PMID:36923454 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Rather than driving myofiber necrosis, complement activation may be secondary to muscle injury."
The trialists' own reading of their negative result, and the position this discussion turns on: complement deposition may be a consequence of the injury rather than its cause, which is precisely the direction an effector edge would have asserted.
⚙

Pathophysiology

14
Reduced Statin Transporter Function
Reduced-function variants of two transporters that between them govern how much of an administered dose reaches the systemic circulation. OATP1B1, encoded by SLCO1B1, is the hepatic uptake transporter, so reduced function lowers hepatic extraction of the drug. BCRP, encoded by ABCG2, is an efflux transporter whose contribution to statin disposition is not hepatic extraction, so reduced function is recorded here as raising systemic availability without this entry asserting which compartment it acts in - the source measures accumulation, not its site. This node is host susceptibility rather than drug action: it injures nothing by itself, and its only consequence in this pathograph is the concentration reached by a given dose. It is absent in most people who develop toxicity, so the chain below it runs with or without it.
SLCO1B1 hgnc:10959 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves SLCO1B1 (hgnc:10959). hgnc:10959 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. ABCG2 hgnc:74 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves ABCG2 (hgnc:74). hgnc:74 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
xenobiotic transmembrane transporter activity GO:0042910 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this molecular function This pathophysiological event involves decreased xenobiotic transmembrane transporter activity (GO:0042910). GO:0042910 is a molecular function from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure Reduced uptake and efflux capacity leave more drug in the systemic circulation for a given administered dose.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39796117 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"DF and PF volunteers for SLCO1B1 showed an increase in AUC72h/DW"
Measures reduced OATP1B1 function raising drug exposure, so each of the two transporters bound on this node has a measurement of its own rather than one standing in for both.
Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure
The systemic statin concentration actually reached, which is what determines delivery of drug to tissues other than liver. Set by the molecule and dose, by interacting drugs that inhibit statin metabolism or transport, by renal and hepatic function, and by the transporter genotype above. Higher exposure is the quantity that scales with risk in the dose-comparison and genotype literature alike.
Downstream
HMG-CoA Reductase Inhibition in Skeletal Muscle Drug reaching skeletal muscle engages the same enzyme it engages in liver; the muscle is not a target tissue for the therapeutic effect, and this engagement is the whole of the off-target lesion.
Impaired Glucose Regulation Treatment is followed by a shift in glucose handling that scales with treatment intensity. The intermediates are marked unknown because the step between drug exposure and dysglycemia is not established: insulin resistance and impaired beta-cell function are both proposed and neither is demonstrated here.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:36694885 NO_EVIDENCE REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Numerous risk factors and pathological mechanisms have been proposed for the development of SRM, but a detailed mechanism has yet to be determined."
Graded NO_EVIDENCE because it does not bear on this node's claim: it is the review's statement that the mechanism downstream of exposure is unsettled. Carried because a reader of the chain below needs to know the field regards it as incompletely determined, not because it supports the chain.
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT BACKGROUND In Vitro
"Genome-wide association studies for statin myopathy have pointed to polymorphisms in the hepatic organic anion transporter that may influence circulating levels of statins (7)."
States that transporter genotype influences the circulating statin level, which is this node's claim: what reaches tissue is the systemic concentration, and it varies with host handling of the drug.
PMID:38751493 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"Some statins (atorvastatin, simvastatin, and lovastatin) are metabolized by the enzyme CYP3A4 in the liver. When it is used in large doses or in combination with CYP3A4 inhibitors (such as macrolides), both of the drug concentration and the liver load increase significantly, which can directly..."
Supports the drug-interaction determinant this node names: co-administration of a CYP3A4 inhibitor raises the concentration reached. This is the most clinically actionable route to elevated exposure.
HMG-CoA Reductase Inhibition in Skeletal Muscle
Competitive inhibition of HMG-CoA reductase within the myocyte. The enzyme and the binding are the same as in the hepatocyte, where the identical inhibition is the intended therapeutic action - which is why no host variant is required anywhere in this chain, and why the toxicity cannot be separated from the drug's mechanism of benefit.
skeletal muscle fiber CL:0008002 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves skeletal muscle fiber (CL:0008002). CL:0008002 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
HMGCR hgnc:5006 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves HMGCR (hgnc:5006). hgnc:5006 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
HMG-CoA reductase activity in skeletal muscle GO:0004420 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this molecular function This pathophysiological event involves decreased HMG-CoA reductase activity in skeletal muscle, annotated with hydroxymethylglutaryl-CoA reductase (NADPH) activity (GO:0004420). GO:0004420 is a molecular function from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Depletion of Mevalonate Pathway Isoprenoid Intermediates HMG-CoA reductase catalyses the committed step of the mevalonate pathway, so inhibiting it depletes every branch product downstream of mevalonate, not only cholesterol.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT INDIRECT BACKGROUND In Vitro
"The etiology of this side effect is not fully understood, but is generally thought to be on-target, that is, related to statins' effects on HMG-CoA reductase."
States that the muscle side effect is on-target - an effect of inhibiting HMG-CoA reductase itself - which is this node's claim. Graded INDIRECT because the sentence reports the field's position rather than measuring enzyme inhibition in myocytes.
Depletion of Mevalonate Pathway Isoprenoid Intermediates
Loss of the non-sterol branch products of the mevalonate pathway within the myocyte - the prenyl donors farnesyl and geranylgeranyl pyrophosphate, and ubiquinone. Which of these matters is the central mechanistic question, and experimental rescue separates them: restoring geranylgeranyl pyrophosphate prevents the injury, while restoring cholesterol or ubiquinone does not.
isoprenoid biosynthetic process GO:0008299 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased isoprenoid biosynthetic process (GO:0008299). GO:0008299 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ubiquinone biosynthetic process GO:0006744 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased ubiquinone biosynthetic process (GO:0006744). GO:0006744 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. cholesterol biosynthetic process GO:0006695 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased cholesterol biosynthetic process (GO:0006695). GO:0006695 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Impaired Protein Geranylgeranylation Depleted geranylgeranyl pyrophosphate is the prenyl donor for geranylgeranylation, so its loss reduces the modification.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT BACKGROUND In Vitro
"It is well known that inhibiting HMG-CoA reductase results in a homeostatic change that preserves the synthesis of mevalonate, so it has been suggested that a decrease in other products of this pathway, such as ubiquinone (CoQ) may underlie muscle toxicity"
States that the pathway's other products fall when the enzyme is inhibited, which is the depletion this node asserts.
Impaired Protein Geranylgeranylation
Reduced geranylgeranylation of small GTPases and other prenylated substrates in the myocyte, the modification that anchors them to membranes and is required for their signalling. Pharmacological dissection places the relevant transferase at geranylgeranyltransferase II rather than type I.
protein geranylgeranylation GO:0018344 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased protein geranylgeranylation (GO:0018344). GO:0018344 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Experimental models
C2C12 myotube simvastatin exposure with pathway-intermediate add-back CELL LINE The add-back and transferase-inhibitor arms intervene directly on geranylgeranylation, which is what isolates this step rather than merely observing it.
Downstream
Myocyte Bioenergetic Failure Blocking geranylgeranyl transfer while the donor is replete reproduces the bioenergetic deficit, which places the transfer step itself in the causal path.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT In Vitro
"These results suggest that blockade of a Rab prenylation event by statins contributes to muscle toxicity."
The paper's own conclusion that statins block a prenylation event and that this contributes to muscle toxicity, which is the reduced modification this node asserts. Rab proteins are geranylgeranyltransferase II substrates, consistent with the transferase this node names.
Myocyte Bioenergetic Failure
Falling cellular energy charge in the statin-exposed muscle fiber, with the accompanying stress, senescence and apoptotic programmes that human muscle biopsy shows after rechallenge. This is the point at which a molecular lesion becomes cell injury, and it is dose-graded rather than all-or-none, which is consistent with the clinical spectrum running from reversible symptoms to overt necrosis.
skeletal muscle fiber CL:0008002 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves skeletal muscle fiber (CL:0008002). CL:0008002 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
muscle cell apoptotic process GO:0010657 ↑ INCREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased muscle cell apoptotic process (GO:0010657). GO:0010657 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Experimental models
C2C12 myotube simvastatin exposure with pathway-intermediate add-back CELL LINE The model reproduces the falling energy charge and the apoptotic programme of this node, but not the fiber necrosis or the clinical syndrome downstream of it.
Downstream
Skeletal Muscle Fiber Necrosis Sustained energy failure and activation of apoptotic and stress programmes in the fiber proceed to frank fiber breakdown when exposure is high enough or prolonged enough.
Myalgia Muscle pain arises at this sub-necrotic level of injury rather than from fiber breakdown: the stratum in which myalgia is commonest is defined by a normal creatine kinase, so muscle pain is reported where fibers are not breaking down. What converts myocyte stress into the sensation of pain is not established, which is why the intermediates are recorded as unknown.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT BACKGROUND In Vitro
"Specifically, we reported that a subset of statins tend to decrease cellular ATP and MTT levels, while leaving intact the mitochondrial gene expression and membrane potential (8)."
Reports the falling cellular energy charge this node asserts, and reports that mitochondrial gene expression and membrane potential were preserved - so this node is an energy deficit and not a demonstrated mitochondrial failure. The sentence restates the authors' earlier work, which quote_role records.
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We also observed that GGPP suppressed the effects of simvastatin on caspase-3/7 and MTT activities, indicating a role for apoptosis as well"
Supports the apoptotic process annotated on this node, and ties it to the same geranylgeranyl-dependent chain: the caspase activation is suppressed by restoring the prenyl donor.
Skeletal Muscle Fiber Necrosis
Breakdown of muscle fibers, releasing creatine kinase and myoglobin into the circulation. This node is the convergence point of the entry: the direct toxic route above and the autoimmune route below both arrive here, which is why creatine kinase elevation and proximal weakness cannot by themselves tell the two apart, and why the course after drug withdrawal is what distinguishes them.
skeletal muscle fiber CL:0008002 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves skeletal muscle fiber (CL:0008002). CL:0008002 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Animal models
Zebrafish lovastatin myofiber toxicity model Zebrafish partially recapitulates Produces loss of myofiber size and integrity in an intact animal, which is the tissue-level outcome the culture model can only infer. It is the only system cited here in which statin exposure damages muscle within a whole organism.
Downstream
Elevated circulating creatine kinase activity Creatine kinase enters the circulation from broken-down fibers.
Proximal muscle weakness Loss of functioning fibers produces objective weakness. Note that the subjective weakness reported in SAMS, where creatine kinase is normal, is not this edge: it arises upstream of necrosis.
Muscle fiber necrosis Fiber breakdown is what a pathologist observes on biopsy as necrotic fibers, which is how this mechanism becomes an examinable finding.
Rhabdomyolysis Extensive fiber breakdown with biochemical evidence of end-organ damage is what the trial literature defines as rhabdomyolysis.
Myoglobin Release into the Circulation Myoglobin held in the fiber enters the bloodstream when the fiber breaks down.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The higher the CK level, the greater the risk for acute metabolic complications, and with a greater release of myoglobin in severe muscle necrosis, the chance of acute kidney injury is very high."
Ties muscle necrosis to both of the outputs this node asserts - creatine kinase level and myoglobin release - and grades them together by severity.
Myoglobin Release into the Circulation
Myoglobin from broken-down muscle fibers entering the bloodstream in quantity, and reaching the glomerulus to be filtered. This is a systemic burden, not yet an injury: it is what makes the kidney the next organ at risk, and it is the step no treatment here can prevent once the muscle has broken down.
Downstream
Myoglobinuria Filtered myoglobin appears in the urine, which is the clinically visible sign of this burden.
Myoglobin-Induced Renal Tubular Injury Myoglobin deposited in the tubule is directly toxic to tubular epithelium.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"the large amounts of myoglobin released from the muscle enter the circulation and get filtered in the glomerulus"
States this node's two steps and no more: release from muscle into the circulation, and glomerular filtration. The tubular injury that follows is the next node.
Myoglobin-Induced Renal Tubular Injury
Injury to the renal tubule by deposited myoglobin. This is the complication that makes rhabdomyolysis an emergency rather than a laboratory abnormality, and it is the reason renal function and urine are assessed alongside creatine kinase. It is kept separate from the release above because it is the only half of the sequence any treatment in this entry can act on.
Downstream
Acute kidney injury
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"to be deposited in the renal tubules, which can result in acute kidney injury"
States this node's own two steps - tubular deposition and the resulting kidney injury - taken from the continuation of the same sentence that supports the release node above.
Statin-Induced HMGCR Autoantigen Exposure
Mechanism confidence: Hypothetical
Statin exposure raises expression of the HMG-CoA reductase protein, the drug's own target, in exposed cells. This is the proposed first step of the autoimmune branch: more antigen available to a susceptible immune system. The step is demonstrated in cultured cells; that it initiates the human antibody response is inferred, and the entry does not claim otherwise.
HMGCR hgnc:5006 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves HMGCR (hgnc:5006). hgnc:5006 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Downstream
Anti-HMGCR Autoantibody Response Increased availability of the autoantigen is the proposed route by which an antibody response against it arises in a susceptible person. The intermediates are unknown: antigen induction is shown in cultured cells, and what carries it from there to a human autoantibody response is not established.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21360500 SUPPORT In Vitro
"RESULTS: Statin exposure induced expression of the ∼200-kd/∼100-kd autoantigens in cultured cells."
Demonstrates the antigen induction this node asserts, in cultured cells, which is why the node is marked HYPOTHETICAL as a step in human disease.
Anti-HMGCR Autoantibody Response
A circulating antibody response against HMG-CoA reductase. The same protein the drug inhibits becomes the target of autoimmunity, which is what makes this stratum self-sustaining: removing the drug removes the inhibition but not the antibody, so the disease continues after withdrawal. Antibody detection is the diagnostic test for this branch.
humoral immune response mediated by circulating immunoglobulin GO:0002455 ↑ INCREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased humoral immune response mediated by circulating immunoglobulin (GO:0002455). GO:0002455 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Autoimmune Myofiber Necrosis and Regeneration The antibody response is accompanied by necrotizing muscle injury that responds to immunosuppression rather than to drug withdrawal, which is the basis for treating it as immune-mediated. The intermediates are recorded as unknown because what the antibody does to the fiber is exactly what is unsettled: the candidate effector was terminal complement, and the one trial that inhibited it in patients was negative.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21360500 SUPPORT In Vitro
"The identity of the ∼100-kd autoantigen was confirmed by immunoprecipitation of in vitro-translated 3-hydroxy-3-methylglutaryl-coenzyme A reductase (HMGCR) protein."
Identifies HMG-CoA reductase as the antigen of the antibody response this node asserts, which is what makes the drug's target and the autoantigen the same protein. Graded IN_VITRO: the experiment is immunoprecipitation of in vitro-translated protein, and patient serum being the reagent does not make it a clinical observation.
Autoimmune Myofiber Necrosis and Regeneration
Myofiber necrosis with regeneration and myophagocytosis, and comparatively sparse lymphocytic inflammation, which is the histological signature that separates this stratum from an inflammatory myositis. Clinically it is progressive symmetrical proximal weakness with markedly raised creatine kinase that persists after the statin is stopped.
skeletal muscle fiber CL:0008002 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves skeletal muscle fiber (CL:0008002). CL:0008002 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Downstream
Skeletal Muscle Fiber Necrosis The autoimmune route converges on the same fiber breakdown as the direct toxic route, which is why both produce creatine kinase release and proximal weakness.
Necrotizing myopathy
Dysphagia Pharyngeal muscle involvement in more severe disease.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21360500 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Regenerating muscle cells express high levels of HMGCR, which may sustain the immune response even after statins are discontinued."
States the mechanism of this node's defining feature: the regenerating fiber itself supplies the antigen, so the process is self-sustaining once the drug is gone. This is why withdrawal is not sufficient treatment in this stratum, and why the node sits downstream of the antibody rather than of drug exposure.
PMID:41010829 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Among patients observed for over 180 days, six out of eight achieved complete remission, with a median of 242 days from treatment initiation, but three relapsed without statin re-challenges."
Relapse in the absence of any re-exposure is the clinical observation that the preceding mechanism predicts, and it is the reason this stratum is managed as an autoimmune disease rather than as a drug effect.
Impaired Glucose Regulation
Mechanism confidence: Hypothetical
A shift in glucose handling during statin treatment, graded by treatment intensity, which in susceptible people crosses into new-onset diabetes. The candidate mechanisms are increased insulin resistance and an adverse effect on beta-cell function. Neither is settled, and neither has been demonstrated in a tissue; what is established is the association with treatment intensity. The mechanism is therefore marked unknown, and no cell type is bound.
glucose homeostasis GO:0042593 ↕ DYSREGULATED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves dysregulated glucose homeostasis (GO:0042593). GO:0042593 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Type II diabetes mellitus Sustained impairment of glucose handling is what is diagnosed as new-onset diabetes when it crosses the diagnostic threshold.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39169014 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"The hyperglycemic state associated with statins can be induced by increased insulin resistance, possibly associated with changes in free fatty acids18, harmful effects on beta cell function, or possibly a combination of the two"
Names both candidate mechanisms for the dysglycemia this node asserts - insulin resistance and beta-cell dysfunction - and names them as possibilities, which is the uncertainty the node's HYPOTHETICAL confidence records.
Hepatocyte Mitochondrial Injury
Mechanism confidence: Provisional
Statin exposure injures hepatocyte mitochondria in a time- and dose-dependent manner in experimental systems, with loss of membrane potential and increased superoxide. This is the proposed basis of the aminotransferase elevation seen clinically; the link from the experimental lesion to the clinical enzyme rise in patients is not established, and most mild elevations are not shown to reflect hepatocellular destruction at all.
hepatocyte CL:0000182 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves hepatocyte (CL:0000182). CL:0000182 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
response to oxidative stress GO:0006979 ↑ INCREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased response to oxidative stress (GO:0006979). GO:0006979 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
mitochondrion GO:0005739 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cellular component This pathophysiological event involves mitochondrion (GO:0005739). GO:0005739 is a cellular component from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Elevated circulating hepatic transaminase concentration Hepatocyte injury releases aminotransferases. The intermediates are recorded as unknown because the mitochondrial lesion is demonstrated in vitro while the clinical enzyme elevation is observed separately, and the two have not been joined in patients.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38751493 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS In Vitro
"Statins can result in a decrease in membrane potential and a significant increase in superoxides in mitochondria, which then lead to serious mitochondrial damage."
States the mitochondrial lesion this node asserts - lost membrane potential and increased superoxide - as reported from experimental work in a review.

Genetic Associations

3
SLCO1B1 (Reduced-function variants of the hepatic uptake transporter OATP1B1 raise systemic statin exposure and with it the risk of muscle injury, most strongly for simvastatin.)
Gene: SLCO1B1 hgnc:10959 gene page HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is SLCO1B1 (hgnc:10959). hgnc:10959 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY variant_origin: GERMLINE
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:18650507 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The odds ratio for myopathy was 4.5 (95% confidence interval [CI], 2.6 to 7.7) per copy of the C allele, and 16.9 (95% CI, 4.7 to 61.1) in CC as compared with TT homozygotes."
A genome-wide study quantifying the allele-dose relationship between this locus and statin-induced myopathy, which is the susceptibility claim recorded here.
PMID:40281622 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Atorvastatin users with the SLCO1B1 rs4149056 variant exhibited a twofold increased risk of SAMS"
A prospective cohort showing the association extends to atorvastatin, with an effect size smaller than the simvastatin figure above, which is why the association is recorded as drug-dependent.
PMID:36833383 NO_EVIDENCE Human Clinical
"In our analyses, we could not confirm an association between the SLCO1B1 c.521T>C genotype and any change in statin therapy or arriving at a stable dose sooner in primary care."
A null result in 1,136 statin users, carried because a reader should see it next to the positive findings above. Graded NO_EVIDENCE rather than REFUTE because its outcome is change of statin dispensing, not documented muscle injury: a patient may change statin for many reasons and may be injured without changing, so the study does not bear on the myopathy association the other two items establish.
Variants (1)
rs4149056
SLCO1B1 c.521T>C, p.Val174Ala. A reduced-function missense variant of OATP1B1. No clinical_significance is recorded because that slot carries ACMG pathogenicity classes, and this is a drug-response and pharmacokinetic susceptibility allele rather than a variant pathogenic for a monogenic disease.
ABCG2 (Reduced-function variants of the efflux transporter BCRP raise systemic exposure to rosuvastatin and are associated with liver-enzyme elevation during treatment.)
Gene: ABCG2 hgnc:74 gene page HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is ABCG2 (hgnc:74). hgnc:74 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY variant_origin: GERMLINE
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40281622 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"RESULTS: Rosuvastatin users carrying the ABCG2 rs2231142 variant had a threefold increased risk of liver enzyme elevation"
A prospective cohort associating this transporter variant with the hepatic rather than the muscle stratum, and specifically for rosuvastatin.
Variants (1)
rs2231142
ABCG2 c.421C>A, p.Gln141Lys. A reduced-function missense variant of BCRP. As above, no ACMG clinical_significance class is recorded for a drug-response allele.
CYP2C9 (Reduced-function variants of the metabolising enzyme CYP2C9 raise exposure to the statins it clears, and CPIC's 2022 statin guideline carries a fluvastatin dose recommendation keyed on CYP2C9 phenotype for the prevention of statin-associated muscle symptoms.)
Gene: CYP2C9 hgnc:2623 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is CYP2C9 (hgnc:2623). hgnc:2623 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY variant_origin: GERMLINE
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:39796117 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Other
"The Clinical Pharmacogenetics Implementation Consortium (CPIC) in its statins guideline of 2022 includes recommendations for dose adjustment according to the pharmacogenetic phenotype of SLCO1B1 (for atorvastatin, simvastatin, rosuvastatin, fluvastatin, pitavastatin, pravastatin, lovastatin),..."
An actionable recommendation keyed on this gene, for the prevention of the same adverse outcome this entry models. quote_role records that the citing study is restating the guideline rather than producing it.
PMID:39796117 NO_EVIDENCE Human Clinical
"No significant differences were found when we analyzed the remaining genes involved in the metabolism or transport of rosuvastatin, but a trend toward an increase in AUC and Cmax was observed in CYP2C9 poor metabolizers (PM) (Table 4)."
Carried to bound the claim rather than to support it. This study measured rosuvastatin, which is not the statin CPIC keys on CYP2C9, and found only a non-significant trend - so it is not evidence for the susceptibility above, and a reader should not take the two items as agreeing.

Medical Actions

5
Withdrawal of the implicated statin
Action: statin discontinuationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is statin discontinuation, annotated with Pharmacotherapy Discontinuation (NCIT:C128535). NCIT:C128535 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Pharmacotherapy Discontinuation NCIT:C128535
Platform: Other
Stopping the drug. For the direct toxic strata this is both the diagnostic manoeuvre and the treatment, because removing the exposure removes the lesion and creatine kinase and symptoms then fall. It is required immediately for severe creatine-kinase elevation, rhabdomyolysis or progressive weakness. It is not sufficient in the autoimmune stratum, where the disease continues without the drug.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure — Removes the exposure that drives the whole pathograph, so every step below it ceases to be driven.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In patients with a CK elevation of more than four times the ULN, and definitely in those with a CK elevation of above 10 times the ULN, even without the muscle symptoms, statin therapy should be stopped."
Gives the thresholds at which withdrawal is indicated, which is the claim this treatment records.
Statin switch, dose reduction or alternate-day dosing
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Platform: Small molecule
Reducing the exposure without abandoning lipid-lowering treatment: changing to a different statin, lowering the dose, dosing on alternate days, or combining a tolerated statin with a non-statin agent. The International Lipid Expert Panel groups these as the SLAP options. They are options rather than an obligatory sequence, and they apply to ordinary muscle symptoms - not to confirmed anti-HMGCR disease, where re-exposure is avoided permanently.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure — Lowers the systemic concentration reached, which is the quantity that scales with risk, while retaining some lipid-lowering effect.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35969116 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"then the SLAP (Switch drugs, Lower dose, Alternate‐day dosing, Polypharmacy) management algorithm"
Names the four exposure-reducing options this treatment record covers, as the expert panel sets them out.
Non-statin lipid-lowering therapy
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: ezetimibe CHEBI:49040 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses ezetimibe (CHEBI:49040). CHEBI:49040 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. bempedoic acid CHEBI:149601 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses bempedoic acid (CHEBI:149601). CHEBI:149601 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Ezetimibe, bempedoic acid or a PCSK9-directed drug, used when the cardiovascular indication cannot be met with a tolerated statin exposure. These treat the underlying lipid disorder and the cardiovascular risk of leaving it untreated; they are not antidotes, and they do nothing for muscle necrosis once it has occurred. They are recorded here because withdrawing a statin has its own cost, which is what makes this entry's management a trade-off rather than a removal.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39497020 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In addition to statins, which have been available for several decades, ezetimibe, bempedoic acid (also as fixed dose combinations), and modulators of proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9 (PCSK9 inhibitors and inclisiran) are additionally very effective approaches to LLT, especially for..."
Establishes these agents as effective lipid-lowering alternatives. Note what it supports: an alternative route to the lipid-lowering goal, not treatment of the muscle or liver injury, which is why no target_mechanisms link is recorded.
Immunosuppression for anti-HMGCR necrotizing myopathy
Action: Immunosuppressive TherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Immunosuppressive Therapy (NCIT:C15261). NCIT:C15261 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15261
Agent: corticosteroid CHEBI:50858 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses corticosteroid (CHEBI:50858). CHEBI:50858 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. methotrexate CHEBI:44185 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses methotrexate (CHEBI:44185). CHEBI:44185 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest. intravenous immunoglobulin NCIT:C80829 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses intravenous immunoglobulin, annotated with Human Immunoglobulin G (NCIT:C80829). NCIT:C80829 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus. rituximab NCIT:C1702 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses rituximab (NCIT:C1702). NCIT:C1702 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Other
Specialist-directed immunosuppression for the autoimmune stratum: glucocorticoids with gradual dose reduction, and a second-line agent such as intravenous immunoglobulin, methotrexate, azathioprine or mycophenolate mofetil, with rituximab reserved for refractory disease. This is the one treatment here aimed at an immune process rather than at drug exposure, and it has no role in any other stratum.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Autoimmune Myofiber Necrosis and Regeneration — Suppresses the immune effector process producing the myofiber necrosis, which is why this stratum responds to immunosuppression where drug withdrawal alone fails.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40249406 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"All patients were treated with steroids (with a gradual dosage reduction), and four of the five received second line immunosuppressive therapy, such as intravenous immunoglobulin, methotrexate, azathioprine, and mycophenolate mofetil."
Documents the agents and the stepped pattern this treatment describes, in patients with the autoimmune stratum. It is a five-patient uncontrolled series, so it records what is given rather than measuring an effect size.
Fluid resuscitation and renal support in rhabdomyolysis
Action: Fluid TherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Fluid Therapy (NCIT:C116537). NCIT:C116537 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C116537
Platform: Other
Intravenous fluid, with electrolyte and renal monitoring, in severe muscle breakdown. Aimed at the kidney rather than at the muscle: it does not reverse fiber necrosis or stop myoglobin entering the circulation, but it is what stands between myoglobin release and established acute kidney injury.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Myoglobin-Induced Renal Tubular Injury — Maintaining urine flow limits the tubular deposition of filtered myoglobin and so the injury it causes. It acts on the tubular injury, not on the release of myoglobin from muscle, which is why those are separate nodes.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38751493 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"The countermeasures for statin-related muscle injury (Figure 1B) and liver dysfunction (Figure 2) including stopping the suspected drug; reducing statin dosage; changing to another lipid-lowering agent; and taking effective treatment measures using liver protectors and hydration treatments, for instance."
Names hydration among the countermeasures for statin-related muscle injury. It is a review listing supportive practice rather than a trial result, which is the weight this record claims; the trials establishing fluid therapy in rhabdomyolysis generally are not specific to statin-induced disease.

Environmental Factors

1
Therapeutic statin exposure
exposure to statin via ingestion ECTO:0900064 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is exposure to statin via ingestion (ECTO:0900064). ECTO:0900064 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Hazard type: CHEMICAL
Route: ORAL
Bound to ECTO:0900064 exposure to statin via ingestion, which carries both the chemical class and the route. ECTO was searched through this repository's own adapter (sqlite:obo:ecto) with the queries l~statin, l~simvastatin, l~atorvastatin, l~rosuvastatin, l~HMG, l~cholesterol, l~lipid-lowering, l~hypolipidemic and l~drug. The class-level ECTO:9000259 exposure to statin also resolves and is the parent of the bound term; agent-specific terms exist for simvastatin, atorvastatin, rosuvastatin and pravastatin, and are not used here because the entry is the drug class rather than one molecule. l~lipid-lowering and l~hypolipidemic return nothing; l~HMG returns only MAXO:0000739 HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor therapy, which names the treatment rather than an exposure, is not an ECTO class, and is from an ontology dismech no longer uses. So no ECTO class for HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor exposure under that name exists.
The exposure is a prescribed medicine taken by mouth, which makes this entry unlike most toxic exposures: the exposure is intended, is usually beneficial, and withdrawing it has its own cardiovascular cost. Risk rises with the molecule and dose, with drugs that inhibit statin metabolism or transport, and with impaired renal or hepatic clearance.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The most common adverse effects associated with statins involve muscle-related toxicity, which results in myalgia, myopathy and rhabdomyolysis."
Establishes the drug class as the agent of this disease's manifestations, which is the entry-level claim this environmental record makes.
Mechanism Target:
TRIGGERS Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure — Taking the drug is what establishes the systemic exposure the rest of the pathograph follows from. Dose determines how much.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21067805 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Compared with two (0·03%) cases of myopathy in patients taking 20 mg simvastatin daily, there were 53 (0·9%) cases in the 80 mg group."
A randomised comparison of two doses of the same drug, so it ties the size of the exposure to the incidence of injury - the exposure-to-burden step this link asserts.

Biochemical Markers

1
Serum creatine kinase (INCREASED)

Reports measured creatine kinase concentrations in the autoimmune stratum. The range is from a selected case series of affected patients, not the distribution across statin users.

Diagnosis

4
Anti-HMGCR antibody testing
Serological detection of antibodies against HMG-CoA reductase. This is the test that separates the autoimmune stratum from the direct toxic one, and the indication for it is the clinical course rather than the severity: proximal weakness or a markedly raised creatine kinase that persists after the statin has been stopped. It is not a screening test for transient muscle pain.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21360500 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"In addition to inducing a self-limited myopathy, statin use is associated with an immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy (IMNM), with autoantibodies that recognize ∼200-kd and ∼100-kd autoantigens."
Identifies the autoantibody whose detection this test rests on, and the distinction from self-limited myopathy that makes the test informative.
Serum creatine kinase measurement
Measurement of serum creatine kinase, used to grade muscle injury and to decide whether to stop the drug. It carries the thresholds on which management turns, but it is a marker of muscle injury rather than a test of drug causation: it is frequently normal in symptomatic patients and rises from exercise and from unrelated muscle disease.
serum creatine kinase activity LOINC:2157-6 Logical Observation Identifiers Names and Codes (LOINC)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"In patients with a CK elevation of more than four times the ULN, and definitely in those with a CK elevation of above 10 times the ULN, even without the muscle symptoms, statin therapy should be stopped."
States the decision thresholds this measurement is performed to establish.
Aminotransferase measurement
Alanine and aspartate aminotransferase, measured at baseline and when hepatic injury is suspected. A trial safety threshold of three times the upper limit of normal prompts reassessment. Aspartate aminotransferase also rises from injured muscle, so a raised value found alongside muscle symptoms is not necessarily hepatic in origin, and this test does not by itself separate the hepatic stratum from the muscle ones.
serum alanine aminotransferase activity LOINC:1742-6 Logical Observation Identifiers Names and Codes (LOINC) serum aspartate aminotransferase activity LOINC:1920-8 Logical Observation Identifiers Names and Codes (LOINC)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38751493 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The deteriorated liver function, especially the increase of serum alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and/or aspartate aminotransferase (AST), is one of the most common side effects of statins."
Names the two analytes this test measures and establishes that their elevation is the manifestation it is performed to detect.
Glycemic measurement
Fasting glucose or glycated hemoglobin, which is how the metabolic stratum is detected. Creatine kinase has no bearing on it, which is part of why that stratum is kept separate: it is found by a different test, on a different schedule, in patients selected by metabolic risk rather than by muscle symptoms.
glycated hemoglobin fraction LOINC:4548-4 Logical Observation Identifiers Names and Codes (LOINC)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39169014 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"NODM was defined as glycated hemoglobin (HbA1c) levels ≥ 6.5% or newly treated with oral hypoglycemic agents or insulin during follow-up."
The measurement and threshold by which the cohort study ascertained new-onset diabetes, which is the test this record describes. Note the definition also admits newly started treatment, so it is not a pure laboratory criterion.
📊

Prevalence

3
Statin-treated patients
Point Prevalence 4000.0 per 100,000 (3000.0–5000.0) >1 in 1,000
3-5% of treated patients, as estimated by the International Lipid Expert Panel. The denominator is people taking a statin, not the general population. This figure is complete statin intolerance - inability to take any statin at any dose - and is not the frequency of any single manifestation modelled here; reported muscle symptoms are considerably more common and causally attributable injury considerably less so.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35969116 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Other
"However, complete statin intolerance occurs in only a small minority of treated patients (estimated prevalence of only 3-5%)."
Source for the intolerance estimate and its denominator.
Statin users
Annual Incidence 0.15 per 100,000 (0.1–0.2) per year 1–9 per 1,000,000 per year Anti-HMGCR IMNM
1-2 cases per million statin users per year. The denominator is statin users, not the general population, and the measure is incidence rather than prevalence, so it must not be compared with the intolerance figure above.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36694885 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"SINAM is an exceptionally uncommon condition, with an occurrence of 1–2 cases per million statin users in a year."
Source for the incidence and its denominator.
Survivors of myocardial infarction randomised to simvastatin 80 mg daily for a mean of 6.7 years
Period Prevalence 879.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000 CK-Defined Myopathy
53 of 6,031 (0.9%) developed myopathy on simvastatin 80 mg against 2 of 6,033 (0.03%) on 20 mg, over a mean 6.7 years in the SEARCH trial. This is a dose-comparison figure within one trial of one statin at a dose no longer in routine use, not an incidence applicable to contemporary statin prescribing.

Related Datasets

1
Patients Experiencing Statin-Induced Myalgia Exhibit a Unique Program of Skeletal Muscle Gene Expression Following Statin Re-challenge geo:GSE97254
Skeletal muscle gene expression profiling comparing biopsies from statin-intolerant patients undergoing statin rechallenge against statin-tolerant controls. The source dataset for the transcriptional signature cited on the myocyte injury node, and one of the few human muscle datasets in which the exposure was deliberately re-applied.
human MICROARRAY n=23
PMID:28771594
Show evidence (1 reference)
GEO:GSE97254 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"To elucidate changes in gene expression associated with statin-induced myalgia, we compared profiles of gene expression in the biopsied skeletal muscle from statin-intolerant patients undergoing statin re-challenge versus those of statin-tolerant controls."
The repository's own description of what the dataset contains and which comparison it was built on.

Clinical Trials

2
NCT04025632 PHASE_II TERMINATED
Randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial of the C5 inhibitor zilucoplan in immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy, registered as terminated. The trial behind this entry's refusal to draw a complement-mediated effector edge in the autoimmune stratum: it was negative despite confirmed target engagement. It enrolled anti-HMGCR and anti-SRP patients together, so it is not confined to the statin-associated subset. The result this entry rests on is the published eight-week primary endpoint in PMID:36923454, which reports the randomised comparison rather than a curtailed interim look.

HP:0008978 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology. Elevated circulating creatine kinase activity HP:0003236 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical trial targets this phenotype This clinical trial targets Elevated circulating creatine kinase activity (HP:0003236). HP:0003236 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.

clinicaltrials:NCT04025632 SUPPORT Other
"The purpose of the study is to evaluate the safety and efficacy of zilucoplan in patients with Immune-Mediated Necrotizing Myopathy (IMNM)."
The registry record establishing the trial's identity and population. Its result is cited from the publication, PMID:36923454, in the complement discussion.
NCT02993406 PHASE_III COMPLETED
CLEAR Outcomes: bempedoic acid against placebo for cardiovascular events in statin-intolerant patients. Relevant here not as a treatment for statin toxicity but as the trial that establishes an alternative route to the lipid-lowering goal for exactly the population this entry describes - people who cannot sustain a statin. That is what makes withdrawal a trade-off rather than a free action.
Show evidence (1 reference)
clinicaltrials:NCT02993406 SUPPORT Other
"The purpose of this study is to determine if treatment with bempedoic acid (ETC-1002) versus placebo decreases the risk of cardiovascular events in participants who have or are at high risk for cardiovascular disease and are statin intolerant."
Establishes the trial's population as statin-intolerant patients and its endpoint as cardiovascular events, which is the sense in which it bears on this entry: it addresses the consequence of stopping the statin, not the toxicity itself.

Experimental Models

1
C2C12 myotube simvastatin exposure with pathway-intermediate add-back CELL_LINE
Differentiated mouse C2C12 myotubes exposed to simvastatin, with mevalonate-pathway intermediates added back one at a time. This is the system that carries the mechanistic spine of this entry: it is where the geranylgeranyl donor is shown to prevent the injury while cholesterol and ubiquinone do not, and where blocking the transferase while the donor is replete reproduces the deficit. Every molecular step between enzyme inhibition and cell injury recorded here is measured in this model and not in human muscle.
myotube CL:0002372 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this experimental model uses this cell type This experimental model uses myotube (CL:0002372). CL:0002372 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Organism
mouse NCBITaxon:10090 NCBI Taxonomy (NCBITaxon) Relation: this experimental model is built in this organism This experimental model is built in mouse, annotated with Mus musculus (NCBITaxon:10090). NCBITaxon:10090 is an organism from the NCBI Taxonomy.
Publication
PMID:21732624
Pathograph links
Impaired Protein Geranylgeranylation perturbs fidelity: moderate observes: molecular The add-back and transferase-inhibitor arms intervene directly on geranylgeranylation, which is what isolates this step rather than merely observing it.
Limitations: A mouse myoblast-derived line differentiated in culture, exposed to a single statin at 10 micromolar for 48 hours. Human myocytes in a treated patient see a far lower concentration over years, and the line carries none of the transporter-dependent exposure variation that governs risk in people.
Divergences from the human mechanism
species mismatch qualifying
The myotubes are mouse. The prenylation chemistry is conserved, but the claim the entry makes is about human skeletal muscle, and no human myocyte experiment is cited for this step.
temporal scope qualifying
A 48-hour exposure at a supratherapeutic concentration stands in for years of therapeutic dosing. It can show that the step occurs; it cannot show the rate at which it occurs in a patient.
Readouts
Cellular ATP after geranylgeranyl pyrophosphate add-back
Direction: RESTORED
Interpretation: Restoring the prenyl donor restores cellular energy, which is what places the loss of geranylgeranylation rather than of cholesterol or ubiquinone in the causal path.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT In Vitro
"While FPP had no efficacy (Supplementary Figure 1), GGPP completely preserved ATP levels in the presence of simvastatin"
The measurement behind this readout, and the discrimination between the two prenyl donors.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT In Vitro
"To determine which of these branches may be contributing to statin toxicity, we treated C2C12 myotubes for 48 hours with 10 µM simvastatin, while simultaneously adding back various pathway intermediates."
The design of the system, which is what makes it informative for the prenylation step rather than for the clinical syndrome.
Myocyte Bioenergetic Failure partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular The model reproduces the falling energy charge and the apoptotic programme of this node, but not the fiber necrosis or the clinical syndrome downstream of it.
Limitations: Reproduces an energy deficit and caspase activation in culture. It does not reproduce muscle pain, which is the commonest clinical manifestation and has no correlate in a myotube, and it does not reproduce the autoimmune stratum at all.
Divergences from the human mechanism
species mismatch qualifying
Mouse myotubes standing in for human skeletal muscle fibers.
boundary omission qualifying
The model contains no circulation, no transporters governing drug delivery, no immune system and no nervous system, so it cannot represent the exposure step above this node or the pain and autoimmune branches beside it.
Readouts
Caspase-3/7 activity after simvastatin exposure
Direction: INCREASED
Interpretation: Programmed cell death is engaged, and is suppressed by restoring the prenyl donor, which links the energy deficit to cell death within the same chain.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We also observed that GGPP suppressed the effects of simvastatin on caspase-3/7 and MTT activities, indicating a role for apoptosis as well"
The measurement behind this readout.
Recorded because three pathophysiology nodes and the edges between them rest on this one system. Without it the entry would state a molecular mechanism for human muscle injury without anywhere saying that a mouse myoblast line at a supratherapeutic concentration is what the claim is measured in.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT BACKGROUND In Vitro
"We previously identified a cellular signature of statin-induced muscle toxicity in the C2C12 myotube."
Establishes this line as the authors' established model of statin muscle toxicity, which is the basis for treating it as informative for the mechanism nodes it is linked to.

Animal Models

1
Zebrafish lovastatin myofiber toxicity model
Larval zebrafish exposed to lovastatin, with myofiber size and integrity read by myosin heavy chain staining. Used in a blinded design to test whether a suppressor identified in culture also protects muscle in a whole organism, which is the step the myotube system cannot take. The model's own validation is that geranylgeraniol supplementation suppresses the toxicity, the same prenyl-donor rescue seen in culture.
Species
Zebrafish
Genotype
wild type
Publication
PMID:21732624
Pathograph links
Skeletal Muscle Fiber Necrosis partially recapitulates fidelity: low observes: tissue Produces loss of myofiber size and integrity in an intact animal, which is the tissue-level outcome the culture model can only infer. It is the only system cited here in which statin exposure damages muscle within a whole organism.
Limitations: A teleost larva exposed to lovastatin in its water, scored on myofiber morphology. It shares no clinical endpoint with the human disease - no creatine kinase, no pain, no proximal distribution - and cannot speak to the autoimmune stratum, which requires an adaptive immune response to a human autoantigen.
Divergences from the human mechanism
species mismatch qualifying
Zebrafish myofibers standing in for human skeletal muscle. The mevalonate pathway is conserved, and the rescue experiment is the evidence that the relevant biology is; the distribution and tempo of human disease are not modelled.
proxy quantity qualifying
The measured quantity is myofiber size and striation on staining. The node's quantity is fiber necrosis releasing creatine kinase and myoglobin, which this model does not measure.
Readouts
Myofiber size and integrity by myosin heavy chain staining
Direction: DECREASED
Interpretation: Loss of myofiber integrity in an intact animal after statin exposure.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT Model Organism
"In a blinded study, we added either 1 or 5 µM lovastatin and 10 µM Gö6976 to zebrafish for 48 hours, and stained for myosin heavy chain to assess myofiber size and integrity."
The design and the measurement behind this readout, including that the scoring was blinded.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:21732624 SUPPORT BACKGROUND Model Organism
"The successful suppression of statin muscle toxicity in zebrafish by geranylgeraniol indicates that this model is a good one for testing our compounds of interest."
The authors' stated basis for treating this model as informative: the prenyl-donor rescue that defines the mechanism in culture also works in the animal.
The passive-transfer mouse model named in the complement discussion's rationale is deliberately not recorded here. That work is cited in this entry only through the zilucoplan trial's framing of it, not from the primary report, so there is no source in this entry from which to state its genotype, readouts or limitations.
