Source YAML

click to show 
name: Statin Toxicity
creation_date: '2026-10-23T15:00:00Z'
description: >-
  Statin toxicity is the adverse-effect spectrum of HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor
  therapy. One drug-target engagement produces it, but the engagement is shared with
  the drug's intended action, so the entry is organised by what the drug does where
  it is not wanted rather than by a lesion the patient carries. Statins inhibit
  HMG-CoA reductase in skeletal muscle as well as in liver, and the resulting loss
  of mevalonate-pathway isoprenoid intermediates - not the loss of cholesterol
  itself - is what experimental rescue points to as the proximate cause of myocyte
  injury. Above that molecular step the entry splits into strata that share almost
  nothing clinically: reported muscle symptoms with a normal creatine kinase,
  creatine-kinase-defined myopathy, rhabdomyolysis with pigment nephropathy, an
  uncommon anti-HMGCR immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy that persists after the
  drug is stopped, aminotransferase elevation, and an excess of new-onset diabetes
  put at around 10% relative risk by randomised trials. The strata carry different denominators, different diagnostic routes
  and opposite management - a careful rechallenge is appropriate for ordinary muscle
  symptoms and is permanently avoided once anti-HMGCR disease is confirmed - so they are
  kept apart as subtypes rather than merged into a single frequency. Host
  susceptibility is pharmacokinetic, not Mendelian: reduced-function alleles of the
  hepatic uptake transporter SLCO1B1 and the efflux transporter ABCG2 raise systemic
  drug exposure, which raises risk without being necessary or sufficient for injury.
categories:
- Toxic Exposure Disorder
- Treatment Toxicity
category: Complex
parents:
- Poisoning
synonyms:
- statin adverse reaction
- statin-associated muscle symptoms
- statin myopathy
- statin-associated myotoxicity
- statin intolerance
disease_term:
  preferred_term: statin toxicity
  term:
    id: MONDO:0033170
    label: statin toxicity
notes: >-
  No GeneReviews chapter names this disease, which is expected: GeneReviews covers
  Mendelian disorders and this is an acquired adverse drug reaction with no causal
  gene. The pharmacogenetic alleles recorded under genetic are susceptibility
  factors for drug exposure, not a monogenic cause, and are typed SUSCEPTIBILITY
  accordingly. The attribution problem is central to this entry and is deliberately
  not modelled as pathophysiology: a muscle symptom arising during statin treatment
  may be caused by the drug, by exercise, by an untreated thyroid or musculoskeletal
  disorder, or by expectation, and blinded rechallenge is what separates these. Only
  the drug-caused branches are given causal edges here.
has_subtypes:
- name: SAMS
  display_name: Statin-associated muscle symptoms (CK normal or minimally raised)
  description: >-
    Symmetrical muscle aching, cramp, stiffness or subjective weakness arising after
    initiation or dose escalation, with creatine kinase normal or only mildly raised
    and no objective weakness. Improves on dechallenge. Distinguished from the strata
    below by the absence of biochemical muscle injury, and managed by switching
    statin, lowering the dose, alternate-day dosing or combination lipid-lowering
    therapy rather than by stopping lipid-lowering treatment altogether.
    Immunosuppression has no role.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:35969116
    reference_title: "Step-by-step diagnosis and management of the nocebo/drucebo effect in statin-associated muscle symptoms patients: a position paper from the International Lipid Expert Panel (ILEP)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      However, complete statin intolerance occurs in only a small minority of
      treated patients (estimated prevalence of only 3-5%).
    explanation: >-
      Separates inability to tolerate any statin from the much more commonly reported
      symptom syndrome, which is the distinction this subtype exists to hold.
  - reference: PMID:25694464
    reference_title: "Statin-associated muscle symptoms: impact on statin therapy-European Atherosclerosis Society Consensus Panel Statement on Assessment, Aetiology and Management."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      Statin-associated muscle symptoms cover a broader range of clinical
      presentations, usually with normal or minimally elevated CK levels, with a
      prevalence of 7-29% in registries and observational studies.
    explanation: >-
      The consensus definition of this stratum, including the normal or minimally
      raised creatine kinase that distinguishes it from the strata below. Its 7-29%
      figure is reported prevalence in registries and observational studies, not
      causally attributable toxicity, which is why no frequency slot is filled from
      it.
- name: CK-Defined Myopathy
  display_name: Statin-induced myopathy and rhabdomyolysis
  description: >-
    Creatine-kinase-defined muscle injury, graded in the clinical literature by
    multiples of the upper limit of normal, extending to rhabdomyolysis with
    myoglobin release and acute kidney injury. Dose- and exposure-dependent, and the
    stratum in which the SEARCH trial's simvastatin dose comparison measured a
    thirty-fold difference in incidence. Requires immediate drug withdrawal and
    assessment of renal function, rather than rechallenge.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      CK levels in severe myopathy and rhabdomyolysis (SRM 4 and 5, respectively)
      are raised above 10 times and 50 times the ULN, respectively
    explanation: >-
      Gives the biochemical grading that separates this stratum from SAMS, where
      creatine kinase is normal or minimally raised.
- name: Anti-HMGCR IMNM
  display_name: Statin-triggered anti-HMGCR immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy
  description: >-
    An uncommon autoimmune myopathy in which statin exposure is followed by an
    antibody response against HMG-CoA reductase itself. Progressive symmetrical
    proximal weakness with markedly raised creatine kinase that persists or worsens
    after the statin is stopped, and biopsy showing myofiber necrosis and
    regeneration with comparatively sparse lymphocytic infiltrate. Diagnosis rests
    on anti-HMGCR serology; treatment is specialist-directed immunosuppression, and
    statin re-exposure is permanently avoided. This is the one stratum where drug
    withdrawal is not sufficient treatment.
  review_notes: >-
    No subtype_term is bound. MONDO:0016098 immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy was
    checked and is deliberately not used: it covers the anti-SRP and statin-naive forms
    as well, and the zilucoplan trial enrolling both antibody groups together is the
    worked illustration of why they are not the same stratum. Binding it would assert
    that this row is that class rather than a part of it. MONDO has no term for the
    statin-triggered anti-HMGCR form, and none of the other four strata has a MONDO
    term either; neither research run supplied subtype identifiers.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:41010829
    reference_title: "Clinical Characteristics and Management of Statin-Associated Anti-3-Hydroxy-3-Methylglutaryl-Coenzyme A Reductase Immune-Mediated Necrotizing Myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The initial creatine kinase (CK) levels ranged from 1438 to over 13,000 IU/L.
    explanation: >-
      Documents the markedly raised creatine kinase that characterises this stratum,
      far above the normal or minimally raised values that define SAMS.
- name: Hepatotoxicity
  display_name: Statin-associated aminotransferase elevation and liver injury
  description: >-
    Usually asymptomatic alanine and aspartate aminotransferase elevation;
    clinically important drug-induced liver injury is uncommon. A trial safety
    threshold of three times the upper limit of normal is used for reassessment, but
    a mild isolated elevation is neither proof of hepatocellular destruction nor
    necessarily of hepatic origin, since aspartate aminotransferase also rises from
    injured muscle.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38751493
    reference_title: "Statin-Associated Liver Dysfunction and Muscle Injury: epidemiology, Mechanisms, and Management Strategies."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      The deteriorated liver function, especially the increase of serum alanine
      aminotransferase (ALT) and/or aspartate aminotransferase (AST), is one of the
      most common side effects of statins.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes aminotransferase elevation as a distinct and common manifestation,
      separate from the muscle strata.
- name: New-Onset Diabetes
  display_name: Statin-associated dysglycemia and new-onset diabetes
  description: >-
    New-onset diabetes in excess during treatment, put at around 10% relative
    risk by randomised trials, greater at higher treatment intensity and concentrated
    in people with baseline metabolic risk. Detected by fasting glucose
    or glycated hemoglobin rather than by creatine kinase, and managed by metabolic
    treatment and benefit-risk reassessment rather than by automatic statin
    cessation.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39169014
    reference_title: "Different diabetogenic effect of statins according to intensity and dose in patients with acute myocardial infarction: a nationwide cohort study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      High-intensity statin was a significant predictor of NODM after adjusting for
      other co-variables
    explanation: >-
      Supports treatment intensity as a determinant of this stratum, which is what
      distinguishes it from the dose-independent attribution problem in SAMS.
pathophysiology:
- name: Reduced Statin Transporter Function
  description: >-
    Reduced-function variants of two transporters that between them govern how much
    of an administered dose reaches the systemic circulation. OATP1B1, encoded by
    SLCO1B1, is the hepatic uptake transporter, so reduced function lowers hepatic
    extraction of the drug. BCRP, encoded by ABCG2, is an efflux transporter whose
    contribution to statin disposition is not hepatic extraction, so reduced function
    is recorded here as raising systemic availability without this entry asserting
    which compartment it acts in - the source measures accumulation, not its site.
    This node is host susceptibility rather than drug action: it injures nothing by
    itself, and its only consequence in this pathograph is the concentration reached
    by a given dose. It is absent in most people who develop toxicity, so the chain
    below it runs with or without it.
  role: modifier
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  genes:
  - preferred_term: SLCO1B1
    term:
      id: hgnc:10959
      label: SLCO1B1
  - preferred_term: ABCG2
    term:
      id: hgnc:74
      label: ABCG2
  molecular_functions:
  - preferred_term: xenobiotic transmembrane transporter activity
    modifier: DECREASED
    term:
      id: GO:0042910
      label: xenobiotic transmembrane transporter activity
  evidence:

    reference_title: "Effect of Genetic Variants on Rosuvastatin Pharmacokinetics in Healthy Volunteers: Involvement of ABCG2, SLCO1B1 and NAT2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      DF and PF volunteers for SLCO1B1 showed an increase in AUC72h/DW
    explanation: >-
      Measures reduced OATP1B1 function raising drug exposure, so each of the two
      transporters bound on this node has a measurement of its own rather than one
      standing in for both.
  downstream:
  - target: Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Reduced uptake and efflux capacity leave more drug in the systemic circulation
      for a given administered dose.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:39796117
      reference_title: "Effect of Genetic Variants on Rosuvastatin Pharmacokinetics in Healthy Volunteers: Involvement of ABCG2, SLCO1B1 and NAT2."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        Genetic analysis showed that decreased (DF) and poor function (PF) volunteers
        for the ABCG2 transporter had higher AUC
      explanation: >-
        A pharmacokinetic measurement in healthy volunteers of the exact step this
        edge asserts - reduced transporter function raising systemic drug exposure -
        rather than of any downstream injury.
  notes: >-
    The two transporters move the drug in opposite directions and are bound to one
    molecular function here because GO's separate canonical activities for them -
    organic-anion transport for OATP1B1, ABC-type xenobiotic efflux for BCRP - would
    split a node whose single claim is the capacity to clear the drug from the
    circulation.
- name: Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure
  description: >-
    The systemic statin concentration actually reached, which is what determines
    delivery of drug to tissues other than liver. Set by the molecule and dose, by
    interacting drugs that inhibit statin metabolism or transport, by renal and
    hepatic function, and by the transporter genotype above. Higher exposure is the
    quantity that scales with risk in the dose-comparison and genotype literature
    alike.
  role: trigger

    explanation: >-

  - reference: PMID:38751493
    reference_title: "Statin-Associated Liver Dysfunction and Muscle Injury: epidemiology, Mechanisms, and Management Strategies."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      Some statins (atorvastatin, simvastatin, and lovastatin) are metabolized by the
      enzyme CYP3A4 in the liver. When it is used in large doses or in combination
      with CYP3A4 inhibitors (such as macrolides), both of the drug concentration and
      the liver load increase significantly, which can directly cause liver damage.
    explanation: >-
      Supports the drug-interaction determinant this node names: co-administration of
      a CYP3A4 inhibitor raises the concentration reached. This is the most clinically
      actionable route to elevated exposure.
  downstream:
  - target: HMG-CoA Reductase Inhibition in Skeletal Muscle
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Drug reaching skeletal muscle engages the same enzyme it engages in liver; the
      muscle is not a target tissue for the therapeutic effect, and this engagement
      is the whole of the off-target lesion.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:21067805
      reference_title: "Intensive lowering of LDL cholesterol with 80 mg versus 20 mg simvastatin daily in 12,064 survivors of myocardial infarction: a double-blind randomised trial."
      supports: SUPPORT
      directness: INDIRECT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        Compared with two (0·03%) cases of myopathy in patients taking 20 mg
        simvastatin daily, there were 53 (0·9%) cases in the 80 mg group.
      explanation: >-
        A randomised comparison of two doses of one statin, so the thirty-fold
        difference in myopathy is attributable to drug exposure. It is graded INDIRECT
        because it measures the clinical endpoint rather than enzyme inhibition in
        muscle, from which the inhibition step is inferred.
  - target: Impaired Glucose Regulation
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Treatment is followed by a shift in glucose handling that scales with treatment
      intensity. The intermediates are marked unknown because the step between drug
      exposure and dysglycemia is not established: insulin resistance and impaired
      beta-cell function are both proposed and neither is demonstrated here.
    evidence:

      explanation: >-

        unknown. The sentence is the cohort study's discussion of prior mechanistic
        work rather than its own result, which quote_role records.
- name: HMG-CoA Reductase Inhibition in Skeletal Muscle
  description: >-
    Competitive inhibition of HMG-CoA reductase within the myocyte. The enzyme and
    the binding are the same as in the hepatocyte, where the identical inhibition is
    the intended therapeutic action - which is why no host variant is required
    anywhere in this chain, and why the toxicity cannot be separated from the drug's
    mechanism of benefit.
  role: central_effector
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR

      label: HMGCR

      label: hydroxymethylglutaryl-CoA reductase (NADPH) activity

  - reference: PMID:21732624
    reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    directness: INDIRECT
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    snippet: >-
      The etiology of this side effect is not fully understood, but is generally
      thought to be on-target, that is, related to statins' effects on HMG-CoA
      reductase.
    explanation: >-
      States that the muscle side effect is on-target - an effect of inhibiting
      HMG-CoA reductase itself - which is this node's claim. Graded INDIRECT because
      the sentence reports the field's position rather than measuring enzyme
      inhibition in myocytes.
  downstream:
  - target: Depletion of Mevalonate Pathway Isoprenoid Intermediates
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      HMG-CoA reductase catalyses the committed step of the mevalonate pathway, so
      inhibiting it depletes every branch product downstream of mevalonate, not only
      cholesterol.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:21732624
      reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      quote_role: BACKGROUND
      snippet: >-
        It is well known that inhibiting HMG-CoA reductase results in a homeostatic
        change that preserves the synthesis of mevalonate, so it has been suggested
        that a decrease in other products of this pathway, such as ubiquinone (CoQ)
        may underlie muscle toxicity
      explanation: >-
        States that enzyme inhibition reduces pathway products other than cholesterol,
        which is the step this edge asserts. The sentence is the paper's framing of
        prior work rather than its own result, so quote_role records that.
  notes: >-
    HMGCR is bound here as a pathophysiology gene descriptor rather than recorded in
    the genetic section, because its relationship to this disease is as the drug's
    pharmacological target and, in one stratum, as an autoantigen. Neither is a
    variant-mediated gene-disease relationship, and no value of relationship_type
    would state it without asserting something false.
- name: Depletion of Mevalonate Pathway Isoprenoid Intermediates
  description: >-
    Loss of the non-sterol branch products of the mevalonate pathway within the
    myocyte - the prenyl donors farnesyl and geranylgeranyl pyrophosphate, and
    ubiquinone. Which of these matters is the central mechanistic question, and
    experimental rescue separates them: restoring geranylgeranyl pyrophosphate
    prevents the injury, while restoring cholesterol or ubiquinone does not.

        ATP levels in the presence of simvastatin
      explanation: >-

        that its depletion is the consequential one of the depleted intermediates.
  notes: >-
    Three branch products are annotated as depleted, and only one of them carries a
    downstream edge. Ubiquinone and cholesterol are annotated because the enzyme's
    inhibition does deplete them, and because the rescue experiment shows that
    replacing either does not prevent myocyte injury. No edge runs from this node to
    the energy deficit by way of ubiquinone: that route is the long-standing
    hypothesis and the experiment argues against it, so the question is carried as a
    discussion rather than drawn as an arrow the entry does not believe. Annotating a
    depletion records what the drug does; an edge would claim which depletion
    matters.

- name: Myocyte Bioenergetic Failure
  description: >-
    Falling cellular energy charge in the statin-exposed muscle fiber, with the
    accompanying stress, senescence and apoptotic programmes that human muscle biopsy
    shows after rechallenge. This is the point at which a molecular lesion becomes
    cell injury, and it is dose-graded rather than all-or-none, which is consistent
    with the clinical spectrum running from reversible symptoms to overt necrosis.

- name: Skeletal Muscle Fiber Necrosis
  description: >-
    Breakdown of muscle fibers, releasing creatine kinase and myoglobin into the
    circulation. This node is the convergence point of the entry: the direct toxic
    route above and the autoimmune route below both arrive here, which is why
    creatine kinase elevation and proximal weakness cannot by themselves tell the two
    apart, and why the course after drug withdrawal is what distinguishes them.

- name: Myoglobin Release into the Circulation
  description: >-
    Myoglobin from broken-down muscle fibers entering the bloodstream in quantity, and
    reaching the glomerulus to be filtered. This is a systemic burden, not yet an
    injury: it is what makes the kidney the next organ at risk, and it is the step no
    treatment here can prevent once the muscle has broken down.

- name: Statin-Induced HMGCR Autoantigen Exposure
  description: >-
    Statin exposure raises expression of the HMG-CoA reductase protein, the drug's own

    inferred, and the entry does not claim otherwise.

- name: Anti-HMGCR Autoantibody Response
  description: >-
    A circulating antibody response against HMG-CoA reductase. The same protein the
    drug inhibits becomes the target of autoimmunity, which is what makes this stratum
    self-sustaining: removing the drug removes the inhibition but not the antibody, so
    the disease continues after withdrawal. Antibody detection is the diagnostic test
    for this branch.

- name: Autoimmune Myofiber Necrosis and Regeneration
  description: >-

- name: Impaired Glucose Regulation
  description: >-
    A shift in glucose handling during statin treatment, graded by treatment
    intensity, which in susceptible people crosses into new-onset diabetes. The
    candidate mechanisms are increased insulin resistance and an adverse effect on
    beta-cell function. Neither is settled, and neither has been demonstrated in a
    tissue; what is established is the association with treatment intensity. The
    mechanism is therefore marked unknown, and no cell type is bound.

- name: Hepatocyte Mitochondrial Injury
  description: >-
    Statin exposure injures hepatocyte mitochondria in a time- and dose-dependent
    manner in experimental systems, with loss of membrane potential and increased
    superoxide. This is the proposed basis of the aminotransferase elevation seen
    clinically; the link from the experimental lesion to the clinical enzyme rise in
    patients is not established, and most mild elevations are not shown to reflect
    hepatocellular destruction at all.

    oxidative-stress process is annotated because the cited source reports increased
    mitochondrial superoxide specifically. Two neighbouring modules were considered
    and rejected: sarcolemmal_fragility_myofiber_necrosis is scoped to mechanical
    fragility of the dystrophin complex rather than toxic metabolic injury, and
    drug_induced_nephrotoxicity begins with tubular uptake of the drug, whereas the
    nephrotoxin in this entry is myoglobin rather than the statin.
phenotypes:
- category: Clinical
  name: Myalgia
  description: >-
    Muscle aching, usually symmetrical and proximal, arising after statin initiation
    or dose escalation and improving on withdrawal. The commonest reported adverse
    effect and the commonest reason for discontinuation, and the one where attribution
    is hardest, because creatine kinase is typically normal and exercise, intercurrent
    illness and expectation produce the same complaint.
  subtype: SAMS
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Myalgia
    term:
      id: HP:0003326
      label: Myalgia
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:28771594
    reference_title: "Patients experiencing statin-induced myalgia exhibit a unique program of skeletal muscle gene expression following statin re-challenge."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Although statins are generally well tolerated, up to ten percent of
      statin-treated patients experience myalgia symptoms, defined as muscle pain
      without elevated creatinine phosphokinase (CPK) levels.
    explanation: >-
      States both the phenotype and the feature that defines this stratum - muscle
      pain without creatine kinase elevation. No frequency slot is filled from the
      ten percent figure: it counts reported symptoms rather than symptoms shown to
      be caused by the drug, and blinded rechallenge shows those are not the same
      quantity.
- category: Clinical
  name: Proximal muscle weakness
  description: >-
    Weakness of the proximal limb muscles, impairing rising from a chair, climbing
    stairs and lifting the arms. Subjective weakness occurs in ordinary muscle
    symptoms, but objective and progressive proximal weakness, particularly when it
    continues after the statin is stopped, is what should prompt evaluation for
    anti-HMGCR disease.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Proximal muscle weakness
    term:
      id: HP:0003701
      label: Proximal muscle weakness
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:21360500
    reference_title: "Autoantibodies against 3-hydroxy-3-methylglutaryl-coenzyme A reductase in patients with statin-associated autoimmune myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Given that these patients had proximal muscle weakness, elevated CK levels, MHC
      I expression on non-necrotic muscle fibers, autoantibodies, and responded to
      immunosuppression, we concluded that these patients had an IMNM.
    explanation: >-
      Documents proximal weakness as a defining clinical feature of the autoimmune
      stratum, alongside the antibody and the treatment response.
- category: Laboratory
  name: Elevated circulating creatine kinase activity
  description: >-
    Raised serum creatine kinase, the marker of muscle fiber injury. It is graded by
    multiples of the upper limit of normal and is the axis on which the muscle strata
    are separated, but it is neither sensitive nor specific for drug causation: it is
    commonly normal in symptomatic patients, and it rises from exercise and from other
    muscle disease.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Elevated circulating creatine kinase activity
    term:
      id: HP:0003236
      label: Elevated circulating creatine kinase activity
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      In patients with a CK elevation of more than four times the ULN, and definitely
      in those with a CK elevation of above 10 times the ULN, even without the muscle
      symptoms, statin therapy should be stopped.
    explanation: >-
      Gives the thresholds at which the raised enzyme changes management, which is the
      sense in which this laboratory phenotype is graded here.
- category: Clinical
  name: Rhabdomyolysis
  description: >-
    Extensive muscle breakdown with markedly raised creatine kinase and biochemical
    evidence of end-organ damage. The severe end of the direct toxic spectrum,
    strongly exposure-dependent, and the manifestation that requires immediate drug
    withdrawal and renal assessment.
  subtype: CK-Defined Myopathy
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Rhabdomyolysis
    term:
      id: HP:0003201
      label: Rhabdomyolysis
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      The most common adverse effects associated with statins involve muscle-related
      toxicity, which results in myalgia, myopathy and rhabdomyolysis.
    explanation: >-
      Places rhabdomyolysis within the muscle-toxicity spectrum of this drug class.
- category: Pathological
  name: Muscle fiber necrosis
  description: >-
    Necrosis of skeletal muscle fibers on biopsy. In the autoimmune stratum it is
    accompanied by regeneration and myophagocytosis with comparatively sparse
    lymphocytic infiltrate, which is what distinguishes it histologically from an
    inflammatory myositis.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Muscle fiber necrosis
    term:
      id: HP:0003713
      label: Muscle fiber necrosis
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:21360500
    reference_title: "Autoantibodies against 3-hydroxy-3-methylglutaryl-coenzyme A reductase in patients with statin-associated autoimmune myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    snippet: >-
      In addition to inducing a self-limited myopathy, statin use is associated with
      an immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy (IMNM), with autoantibodies that
      recognize ∼200-kd and ∼100-kd autoantigens.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes the necrotizing muscle pathology of the autoimmune stratum and
      distinguishes it from the self-limited myopathy of the direct toxic route.
- category: Clinical
  name: Necrotizing myopathy
  description: >-
    An immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy triggered by statin exposure, with
    progressive proximal weakness and markedly raised creatine kinase persisting after
    the drug is withdrawn. Rare relative to the scale of statin use, and the only
    manifestation here that requires immunosuppression.
  subtype: Anti-HMGCR IMNM
  frequency: VERY_RARE
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Necrotizing myopathy
    term:
      id: HP:0008978
      label: Necrotizing myopathy
    clinical_course: PROGRESSIVE
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      SINAM is an exceptionally uncommon condition, with an occurrence of 1–2 cases
      per million statin users in a year.
    explanation: >-
      Supports the rarity recorded in frequency, counted against a denominator of
      statin users rather than of the general population.
- category: Laboratory
  name: Myoglobinuria
  description: >-
    Myoglobin in the urine from massive muscle breakdown, clinically evident as dark
    urine, and the mediator of the renal injury of rhabdomyolysis.
  subtype: CK-Defined Myopathy
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Myoglobinuria
    term:
      id: HP:0002913
      label: Myoglobinuria
  evidence:

    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: >-
      Hence, CK levels should be urgently checked in patients complaining of muscle
      symptoms or highly coloured urine (suggestive of myoglobinuria) in order to
      avoid severe adverse effects, despite their occurrence being very low.
    explanation: >-
      Records myoglobinuria as a recognised manifestation, names the clinical sign
      that suggests it, and states its rarity.
- category: Clinical
  name: Acute kidney injury
  description: >-
    Acute renal failure from filtered myoglobin injuring the tubule, the complication
    that makes severe statin-induced rhabdomyolysis an emergency.
  subtype: CK-Defined Myopathy
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Acute kidney injury
    term:
      id: HP:0001919
      label: Acute kidney injury
    temporality: ACUTE
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      The higher the CK level, the greater the risk for acute metabolic
      complications, and with a greater release of myoglobin in severe muscle
      necrosis, the chance of acute kidney injury is very high.
    explanation: >-
      Supports acute kidney injury as a consequence of severe muscle necrosis, graded
      by the amount of myoglobin released.
- category: Clinical
  name: Dysphagia
  description: >-
    Swallowing difficulty from pharyngeal muscle involvement in more severe
    anti-HMGCR disease.
  subtype: Anti-HMGCR IMNM
  phenotype_term:

      The authors' own observation of dysphagia in this anti-HMGCR series. The quote

      the authors take from other large cohorts, so quoting the whole sentence would
      present somebody else's frequency as this paper's finding. No frequency slot is
      filled here for the same reason.
- category: Laboratory
  name: Elevated circulating hepatic transaminase concentration
  description: >-
    Raised alanine and aspartate aminotransferase, usually asymptomatic and often
    transient. A mild isolated rise does not establish hepatocellular injury, and
    aspartate aminotransferase also rises from injured muscle, so an elevation found
    alongside muscle symptoms is not necessarily hepatic in origin.
  subtype: Hepatotoxicity
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Elevated circulating hepatic transaminase concentration

      aminotransferase (ALT) and/or aspartate aminotransferase (AST), is one of the
      most common side effects of statins.
    explanation: >-
      Supports aminotransferase elevation as a common manifestation of this drug

- category: Clinical
  name: Type II diabetes mellitus
  description: >-
    New-onset type 2 diabetes, in excess during statin treatment and more so at
    higher treatment intensity. Weighed against the cardiovascular benefit
    rather than treated as a reason for automatic withdrawal.
  subtype: New-Onset Diabetes

    explanation: >-

      trials rather than to the citing cohort, which had no statin-free arm and so
      could not establish an excess over no treatment. quote_role records that the

genetic:
- name: SLCO1B1
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: SLCO1B1
    term:
      id: hgnc:10959
      label: SLCO1B1
  presence: PRESENT
  relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY
  association: >-
    Reduced-function variants of the hepatic uptake transporter OATP1B1 raise systemic
    statin exposure and with it the risk of muscle injury, most strongly for
    simvastatin.
  variant_origin: GERMLINE
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:18650507
    reference_title: "SLCO1B1 variants and statin-induced myopathy--a genomewide study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The odds ratio for myopathy was 4.5 (95% confidence interval [CI], 2.6 to 7.7)
      per copy of the C allele, and 16.9 (95% CI, 4.7 to 61.1) in CC as compared with
      TT homozygotes.
    explanation: >-
      A genome-wide study quantifying the allele-dose relationship between this locus
      and statin-induced myopathy, which is the susceptibility claim recorded here.
  - reference: PMID:40281622
    reference_title: "Pharmacogenomic insights into atorvastatin and rosuvastatin adverse effects: a prospective observational study in the UAE's multiethnic population."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Atorvastatin users with the SLCO1B1 rs4149056 variant exhibited a twofold
      increased risk of SAMS
    explanation: >-
      A prospective cohort showing the association extends to atorvastatin, with an
      effect size smaller than the simvastatin figure above, which is why the
      association is recorded as drug-dependent.
  - reference: PMID:36833383
    reference_title: "Predictive Value of SLCO1B1 c.521T>C Polymorphism on Observed Changes in the Treatment of 1136 Statin-Users."
    supports: NO_EVIDENCE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      In our analyses, we could not confirm an association between the SLCO1B1
      c.521T>C genotype and any change in statin therapy or arriving at a stable dose
      sooner in primary care.
    explanation: >-
      A null result in 1,136 statin users, carried because a reader should see it next
      to the positive findings above. Graded NO_EVIDENCE rather than REFUTE because
      its outcome is change of statin dispensing, not documented muscle injury: a
      patient may change statin for many reasons and may be injured without changing,
      so the study does not bear on the myopathy association the other two items
      establish.
  notes: >-
    Typed SUSCEPTIBILITY rather than CAUSATIVE deliberately. The variant is neither
    necessary nor sufficient: most people who develop statin myopathy do not carry it,
    and most carriers tolerate statins. It is a pharmacokinetic risk allele whose
    effect size depends on which statin is prescribed, not a disease-causing mutation.
    A Dutch cohort of 1,136 statin users could not confirm an association between this
    genotype and change of statin therapy, but used dispensing change as the outcome
    rather than documented muscle injury, so it does not contradict the myopathy
    association above.
  variants:
  - name: rs4149056
    description: >-
      SLCO1B1 c.521T>C, p.Val174Ala. A reduced-function missense variant of OATP1B1.
      No clinical_significance is recorded because that slot carries ACMG
      pathogenicity classes, and this is a drug-response and pharmacokinetic
      susceptibility allele rather than a variant pathogenic for a monogenic disease.
- name: ABCG2
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: ABCG2
    term:
      id: hgnc:74
      label: ABCG2
  presence: PRESENT
  relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY
  association: >-
    Reduced-function variants of the efflux transporter BCRP raise systemic exposure
    to rosuvastatin and are associated with liver-enzyme elevation during treatment.
  variant_origin: GERMLINE
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40281622
    reference_title: "Pharmacogenomic insights into atorvastatin and rosuvastatin adverse effects: a prospective observational study in the UAE's multiethnic population."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      RESULTS: Rosuvastatin users carrying the ABCG2 rs2231142 variant had a threefold
      increased risk of liver enzyme elevation
    explanation: >-
      A prospective cohort associating this transporter variant with the hepatic
      rather than the muscle stratum, and specifically for rosuvastatin.
  notes: >-
    Note the stratum: this allele is associated with liver-enzyme elevation on
    rosuvastatin, where SLCO1B1 rs4149056 is associated with muscle symptoms. The two
    are not interchangeable risk markers, and neither predicts the autoimmune
    stratum.
  variants:
  - name: rs2231142
    description: >-
      ABCG2 c.421C>A, p.Gln141Lys. A reduced-function missense variant of BCRP. As
      above, no ACMG clinical_significance class is recorded for a drug-response
      allele.
- name: CYP2C9
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: CYP2C9
    term:
      id: hgnc:2623
      label: CYP2C9
  presence: PRESENT
  relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY
  association: >-
    Reduced-function variants of the metabolising enzyme CYP2C9 raise exposure to the
    statins it clears, and CPIC's 2022 statin guideline carries a fluvastatin dose
    recommendation keyed on CYP2C9 phenotype for the prevention of statin-associated
    muscle symptoms.
  variant_origin: GERMLINE
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39796117
    reference_title: "Effect of Genetic Variants on Rosuvastatin Pharmacokinetics in Healthy Volunteers: Involvement of ABCG2, SLCO1B1 and NAT2."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    snippet: >-
      The Clinical Pharmacogenetics Implementation Consortium (CPIC) in its statins
      guideline of 2022 includes recommendations for dose adjustment according to the
      pharmacogenetic phenotype of SLCO1B1 (for atorvastatin, simvastatin,
      rosuvastatin, fluvastatin, pitavastatin, pravastatin, lovastatin), ABCG2 (for
      rosuvastatin) and CYP2C9 (for fluvastatin) to prevent SAMS
    explanation: >-
      An actionable recommendation keyed on this gene, for the prevention of the same
      adverse outcome this entry models. quote_role records that the citing study is
      restating the guideline rather than producing it.
  - reference: PMID:39796117
    reference_title: "Effect of Genetic Variants on Rosuvastatin Pharmacokinetics in Healthy Volunteers: Involvement of ABCG2, SLCO1B1 and NAT2."
    supports: NO_EVIDENCE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      No significant differences were found when we analyzed the remaining genes
      involved in the metabolism or transport of rosuvastatin, but a trend toward an
      increase in AUC and Cmax was observed in CYP2C9 poor metabolizers (PM) (Table 4).
    explanation: >-
      Carried to bound the claim rather than to support it. This study measured
      rosuvastatin, which is not the statin CPIC keys on CYP2C9, and found only a
      non-significant trend - so it is not evidence for the susceptibility above, and a
      reader should not take the two items as agreeing.
  notes: >-
    Recorded as a third susceptibility gene, and narrower than the other two: the
    actionable claim is statin-specific. CPIC keys CYP2C9 on fluvastatin, where it keys
    SLCO1B1 on seven statins and ABCG2 on rosuvastatin, so a CYP2C9 phenotype says
    nothing about risk on the statins most commonly prescribed. The same cited study
    found only a non-significant trend for CYP2C9 poor metabolizers on rosuvastatin,
    which is consistent with that: it tested the wrong statin for this gene.
environmental:
- name: Therapeutic statin exposure
  description: >-
    The exposure is a prescribed medicine taken by mouth, which makes this entry
    unlike most toxic exposures: the exposure is intended, is usually beneficial, and
    withdrawing it has its own cardiovascular cost. Risk rises with the molecule and
    dose, with drugs that inhibit statin metabolism or transport, and with impaired
    renal or hepatic clearance.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: exposure to statin via ingestion
    term:
      id: ECTO:0900064
      label: exposure to statin via ingestion
  exposure_classifications:
    hazard_agent_type:
    - classification_value: CHEMICAL
    exposure_route:
    - classification_value: ORAL
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure
    environmental_effect: TRIGGERS
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Taking the drug is what establishes the systemic exposure the rest of the
      pathograph follows from. Dose determines how much.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:21067805
      reference_title: "Intensive lowering of LDL cholesterol with 80 mg versus 20 mg simvastatin daily in 12,064 survivors of myocardial infarction: a double-blind randomised trial."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        Compared with two (0·03%) cases of myopathy in patients taking 20 mg
        simvastatin daily, there were 53 (0·9%) cases in the 80 mg group.
      explanation: >-
        A randomised comparison of two doses of the same drug, so it ties the size of
        the exposure to the incidence of injury - the exposure-to-burden step this
        link asserts.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      The most common adverse effects associated with statins involve muscle-related
      toxicity, which results in myalgia, myopathy and rhabdomyolysis.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes the drug class as the agent of this disease's manifestations, which
      is the entry-level claim this environmental record makes.
  notes: >-
    Bound to ECTO:0900064 exposure to statin via ingestion, which carries both the
    chemical class and the route. ECTO was searched through this repository's own
    adapter (sqlite:obo:ecto) with the queries l~statin, l~simvastatin,
    l~atorvastatin, l~rosuvastatin, l~HMG, l~cholesterol, l~lipid-lowering,
    l~hypolipidemic and l~drug. The class-level ECTO:9000259 exposure to statin also
    resolves and is the parent of the bound term; agent-specific terms exist for
    simvastatin, atorvastatin, rosuvastatin and pravastatin, and are not used here
    because the entry is the drug class rather than one molecule. l~lipid-lowering
    and l~hypolipidemic return nothing; l~HMG returns only MAXO:0000739 HMG-CoA
    reductase inhibitor therapy, which names the treatment rather than an exposure,
    is not an ECTO class, and is from an ontology dismech no longer uses. So no
    ECTO class for HMG-CoA reductase inhibitor exposure under that name exists.
diagnosis:
- name: Anti-HMGCR antibody testing
  description: >-
    Serological detection of antibodies against HMG-CoA reductase. This is the test
    that separates the autoimmune stratum from the direct toxic one, and the
    indication for it is the clinical course rather than the severity: proximal
    weakness or a markedly raised creatine kinase that persists after the statin has
    been stopped. It is not a screening test for transient muscle pain.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:21360500
    reference_title: "Autoantibodies against 3-hydroxy-3-methylglutaryl-coenzyme A reductase in patients with statin-associated autoimmune myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    snippet: >-
      In addition to inducing a self-limited myopathy, statin use is associated with
      an immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy (IMNM), with autoantibodies that
      recognize ∼200-kd and ∼100-kd autoantigens.
    explanation: >-
      Identifies the autoantibody whose detection this test rests on, and the
      distinction from self-limited myopathy that makes the test informative.
- name: Serum creatine kinase measurement
  description: >-
    Measurement of serum creatine kinase, used to grade muscle injury and to decide
    whether to stop the drug. It carries the thresholds on which management turns,
    but it is a marker of muscle injury rather than a test of drug causation: it is
    frequently normal in symptomatic patients and rises from exercise and from
    unrelated muscle disease.
  measurements:
  - preferred_term: serum creatine kinase activity
    term:
      id: LOINC:2157-6
      label: Creatine kinase [Enzymatic activity/volume] in Serum or Plasma
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      In patients with a CK elevation of more than four times the ULN, and definitely
      in those with a CK elevation of above 10 times the ULN, even without the muscle
      symptoms, statin therapy should be stopped.
    explanation: >-
      States the decision thresholds this measurement is performed to establish.
- name: Aminotransferase measurement
  description: >-
    Alanine and aspartate aminotransferase, measured at baseline and when hepatic
    injury is suspected. A trial safety threshold of three times the upper limit of
    normal prompts reassessment. Aspartate aminotransferase also rises from injured
    muscle, so a raised value found alongside muscle symptoms is not necessarily
    hepatic in origin, and this test does not by itself separate the hepatic stratum
    from the muscle ones.
  measurements:
  - preferred_term: serum alanine aminotransferase activity
    term:
      id: LOINC:1742-6
      label: Alanine aminotransferase [Enzymatic activity/volume] in Serum or Plasma
  - preferred_term: serum aspartate aminotransferase activity
    term:
      id: LOINC:1920-8
      label: Aspartate aminotransferase [Enzymatic activity/volume] in Serum or Plasma
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38751493
    reference_title: "Statin-Associated Liver Dysfunction and Muscle Injury: epidemiology, Mechanisms, and Management Strategies."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      The deteriorated liver function, especially the increase of serum alanine
      aminotransferase (ALT) and/or aspartate aminotransferase (AST), is one of the
      most common side effects of statins.
    explanation: >-
      Names the two analytes this test measures and establishes that their elevation is
      the manifestation it is performed to detect.
- name: Glycemic measurement
  description: >-
    Fasting glucose or glycated hemoglobin, which is how the metabolic stratum is
    detected. Creatine kinase has no bearing on it, which is part of why that stratum
    is kept separate: it is found by a different test, on a different schedule, in
    patients selected by metabolic risk rather than by muscle symptoms.
  measurements:
  - preferred_term: glycated hemoglobin fraction
    term:
      id: LOINC:4548-4
      label: Hemoglobin A1c/Hemoglobin.total in Blood
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39169014
    reference_title: "Different diabetogenic effect of statins according to intensity and dose in patients with acute myocardial infarction: a nationwide cohort study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      NODM was defined as glycated hemoglobin (HbA1c) levels ≥ 6.5% or newly treated
      with oral hypoglycemic agents or insulin during follow-up.
    explanation: >-
      The measurement and threshold by which the cohort study ascertained new-onset
      diabetes, which is the test this record describes. Note the definition also
      admits newly started treatment, so it is not a pure laboratory criterion.
biochemical:
- name: Serum creatine kinase
  biomarker_term:
    preferred_term: creatine kinase
    term:
      id: NCIT:C113245
      label: Creatine Kinase
  loinc_term:
    id: LOINC:2157-6
    label: Creatine kinase [Enzymatic activity/volume] in Serum or Plasma
  presence: INCREASED
  notes: >-
    No reference_ranges block is written here. A population reference interval for
    creatine kinase is not specific to this disease, and the figures that are specific
    to it are decision thresholds expressed as multiples of the upper limit of normal
    rather than absolute concentrations, so they cannot be recorded as
    interpretation bands without inventing the anchor they multiply.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:41010829
    reference_title: "Clinical Characteristics and Management of Statin-Associated Anti-3-Hydroxy-3-Methylglutaryl-Coenzyme A Reductase Immune-Mediated Necrotizing Myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The initial creatine kinase (CK) levels ranged from 1438 to over 13,000 IU/L.
    explanation: >-
      Reports measured creatine kinase concentrations in the autoimmune stratum. The
      range is from a selected case series of affected patients, not the distribution
      across statin users.
treatments:
- name: Withdrawal of the implicated statin
  description: >-
    Stopping the drug. For the direct toxic strata this is both the diagnostic
    manoeuvre and the treatment, because removing the exposure removes the lesion and
    creatine kinase and symptoms then fall. It is required immediately for severe
    creatine-kinase elevation, rhabdomyolysis or progressive weakness. It is not
    sufficient in the autoimmune stratum, where the disease continues without the
    drug.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: statin discontinuation
    term:
      id: NCIT:C128535
      label: Pharmacotherapy Discontinuation
  therapeutic_modality: OTHER
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure
    treatment_effect: INHIBITS
    description: >-
      Removes the exposure that drives the whole pathograph, so every step below it
      ceases to be driven.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36694885
    reference_title: "Statin-related Muscle Toxicity: An Evidence-based Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      In patients with a CK elevation of more than four times the ULN, and definitely
      in those with a CK elevation of above 10 times the ULN, even without the muscle
      symptoms, statin therapy should be stopped.
    explanation: >-
      Gives the thresholds at which withdrawal is indicated, which is the claim this
      treatment records.
- name: Statin switch, dose reduction or alternate-day dosing
  description: >-
    Reducing the exposure without abandoning lipid-lowering treatment: changing to a
    different statin, lowering the dose, dosing on alternate days, or combining a
    tolerated statin with a non-statin agent. The International Lipid Expert Panel
    groups these as the SLAP options. They are options rather than an obligatory
    sequence, and they apply to ordinary muscle symptoms - not to confirmed
    anti-HMGCR disease, where re-exposure is avoided permanently.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Pharmacotherapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15986
      label: Pharmacotherapy
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Elevated Systemic Statin Exposure
    treatment_effect: INHIBITS
    description: >-
      Lowers the systemic concentration reached, which is the quantity that scales
      with risk, while retaining some lipid-lowering effect.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:35969116
    reference_title: "Step-by-step diagnosis and management of the nocebo/drucebo effect in statin-associated muscle symptoms patients: a position paper from the International Lipid Expert Panel (ILEP)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      then the SLAP (Switch drugs, Lower dose, Alternate‐day dosing, Polypharmacy)
      management algorithm
    explanation: >-
      Names the four exposure-reducing options this treatment record covers, as the
      expert panel sets them out.
- name: Non-statin lipid-lowering therapy
  description: >-
    Ezetimibe, bempedoic acid or a PCSK9-directed drug, used when the cardiovascular
    indication cannot be met with a tolerated statin exposure. These treat the
    underlying lipid disorder and the cardiovascular risk of leaving it untreated;
    they are not antidotes, and they do nothing for muscle necrosis once it has
    occurred. They are recorded here because withdrawing a statin has its own cost,
    which is what makes this entry's management a trade-off rather than a removal.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Pharmacotherapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15986
      label: Pharmacotherapy
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: ezetimibe
      term:
        id: CHEBI:49040
        label: ezetimibe
    - preferred_term: bempedoic acid
      term:
        id: CHEBI:149601
        label: bempedoic acid
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39497020
    reference_title: "2024 Recommendations on the Optimal Use of Lipid-Lowering Therapy in Established Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease and Following Acute Coronary Syndromes: A Position Paper of the International Lipid Expert Panel (ILEP)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      In addition to statins, which have been available for several decades,
      ezetimibe, bempedoic acid (also as fixed dose combinations), and modulators of
      proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9 (PCSK9 inhibitors and inclisiran)
      are additionally very effective approaches to LLT, especially for those at very
      high and extremely high cardiovascular risk.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes these agents as effective lipid-lowering alternatives. Note what it
      supports: an alternative route to the lipid-lowering goal, not treatment of the
      muscle or liver injury, which is why no target_mechanisms link is recorded.
  notes: >-
    Deliberately carries no target_mechanisms link. No node in this pathograph is
    acted on by these drugs: they address the indication the statin was prescribed
    for, which is outside this entry. Linking them to a mechanism node would assert
    they treat the toxicity.
- name: Immunosuppression for anti-HMGCR necrotizing myopathy
  description: >-
    Specialist-directed immunosuppression for the autoimmune stratum: glucocorticoids
    with gradual dose reduction, and a second-line agent such as intravenous
    immunoglobulin, methotrexate, azathioprine or mycophenolate mofetil, with
    rituximab reserved for refractory disease. This is the one treatment here aimed
    at an immune process rather than at drug exposure, and it has no role in any other
    stratum.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Immunosuppressive Therapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15261
      label: Immunosuppressive Therapy
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: corticosteroid
      term:
        id: CHEBI:50858
        label: corticosteroid
    - preferred_term: methotrexate
      term:
        id: CHEBI:44185
        label: methotrexate
    - preferred_term: intravenous immunoglobulin
      term:
        id: NCIT:C80829
        label: Human Immunoglobulin G
    - preferred_term: rituximab
      term:
        id: NCIT:C1702
        label: Rituximab
  therapeutic_modality: OTHER
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Autoimmune Myofiber Necrosis and Regeneration
    treatment_effect: INHIBITS
    description: >-
      Suppresses the immune effector process producing the myofiber necrosis, which
      is why this stratum responds to immunosuppression where drug withdrawal alone
      fails.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:40249406
      reference_title: "Effectiveness of proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9 inhibitors in managing hypercholesterolemia post-statin-associated immune-mediated necrotizing myopathy: report of five cases and literature review."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        All patients were treated with steroids (with a gradual dosage reduction), and
        four of the five received second line immunosuppressive therapy, such as
        intravenous immunoglobulin, methotrexate, azathioprine, and mycophenolate
        mofetil.
      explanation: >-
        Documents the agents and the stepped pattern this treatment describes, in
        patients with the autoimmune stratum. It is a five-patient uncontrolled
        series, so it records what is given rather than measuring an effect size.
  notes: >-
    The case series this cites gave immunosuppression alongside a PCSK9 inhibitor, so
    its clinical improvement cannot be attributed to either arm alone. The record here
    is of the immunosuppressive regimen, and the PCSK9 inhibitor's role in that series
    is lipid-lowering, which is covered by the non-statin therapy record above.
- name: Fluid resuscitation and renal support in rhabdomyolysis
  description: >-
    Intravenous fluid, with electrolyte and renal monitoring, in severe muscle
    breakdown. Aimed at the kidney rather than at the muscle: it does not reverse
    fiber necrosis or stop myoglobin entering the circulation, but it is what stands
    between myoglobin release and established acute kidney injury.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Fluid Therapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C116537
      label: Fluid Therapy
  therapeutic_modality: OTHER
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Myoglobin-Induced Renal Tubular Injury
    treatment_effect: INHIBITS
    description: >-
      Maintaining urine flow limits the tubular deposition of filtered myoglobin and
      so the injury it causes. It acts on the tubular injury, not on the release of
      myoglobin from muscle, which is why those are separate nodes.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38751493
    reference_title: "Statin-Associated Liver Dysfunction and Muscle Injury: epidemiology, Mechanisms, and Management Strategies."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
    snippet: >-
      The countermeasures for statin-related muscle injury (Figure 1B) and liver
      dysfunction (Figure 2) including stopping the suspected drug; reducing statin
      dosage; changing to another lipid-lowering agent; and taking effective treatment
      measures using liver protectors and hydration treatments, for instance.
    explanation: >-
      Names hydration among the countermeasures for statin-related muscle injury. It is
      a review listing supportive practice rather than a trial result, which is the
      weight this record claims; the trials establishing fluid therapy in
      rhabdomyolysis generally are not specific to statin-induced disease.
experimental_models:
- name: C2C12 myotube simvastatin exposure with pathway-intermediate add-back
  description: >-
    Differentiated mouse C2C12 myotubes exposed to simvastatin, with mevalonate-pathway
    intermediates added back one at a time. This is the system that carries the
    mechanistic spine of this entry: it is where the geranylgeranyl donor is shown to
    prevent the injury while cholesterol and ubiquinone do not, and where blocking the
    transferase while the donor is replete reproduces the deficit. Every molecular step
    between enzyme inhibition and cell injury recorded here is measured in this model
    and not in human muscle.
  experimental_model_type: CELL_LINE
  organism:
    preferred_term: mouse
    term:
      id: NCBITaxon:10090
      label: Mus musculus
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: myotube
    term:
      id: CL:0002372
      label: myotube
  publication: PMID:21732624
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Impaired Protein Geranylgeranylation
    relationship: PERTURBS
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: MOLECULAR
    description: >-
      The add-back and transferase-inhibitor arms intervene directly on
      geranylgeranylation, which is what isolates this step rather than merely
      observing it.
    limitations: >-
      A mouse myoblast-derived line differentiated in culture, exposed to a single
      statin at 10 micromolar for 48 hours. Human myocytes in a treated patient see a
      far lower concentration over years, and the line carries none of the
      transporter-dependent exposure variation that governs risk in people.
    divergences:
    - divergence_type: SPECIES_MISMATCH
      materiality: QUALIFYING
      description: >-
        The myotubes are mouse. The prenylation chemistry is conserved, but the claim
        the entry makes is about human skeletal muscle, and no human myocyte
        experiment is cited for this step.
    - divergence_type: TEMPORAL_SCOPE
      materiality: QUALIFYING
      description: >-
        A 48-hour exposure at a supratherapeutic concentration stands in for years of
        therapeutic dosing. It can show that the step occurs; it cannot show the rate
        at which it occurs in a patient.
    readouts:
    - name: Cellular ATP after geranylgeranyl pyrophosphate add-back
      target: Impaired Protein Geranylgeranylation
      direction: RESTORED
      interpretation: >-
        Restoring the prenyl donor restores cellular energy, which is what places the
        loss of geranylgeranylation rather than of cholesterol or ubiquinone in the
        causal path.
      evidence:
      - reference: PMID:21732624
        reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
        supports: SUPPORT
        evidence_source: IN_VITRO
        snippet: >-
          While FPP had no efficacy (Supplementary Figure 1), GGPP completely preserved
          ATP levels in the presence of simvastatin
        explanation: >-
          The measurement behind this readout, and the discrimination between the two
          prenyl donors.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:21732624
      reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      snippet: >-
        To determine which of these branches may be contributing to statin toxicity, we
        treated C2C12 myotubes for 48 hours with 10 µM simvastatin, while
        simultaneously adding back various pathway intermediates.
      explanation: >-
        The design of the system, which is what makes it informative for the
        prenylation step rather than for the clinical syndrome.
  - target: Myocyte Bioenergetic Failure
    relationship: PARTIALLY_RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    description: >-
      The model reproduces the falling energy charge and the apoptotic programme of this
      node, but not the fiber necrosis or the clinical syndrome downstream of it.
    limitations: >-
      Reproduces an energy deficit and caspase activation in culture. It does not
      reproduce muscle pain, which is the commonest clinical manifestation and has no
      correlate in a myotube, and it does not reproduce the autoimmune stratum at all.
    divergences:
    - divergence_type: SPECIES_MISMATCH
      materiality: QUALIFYING
      description: >-
        Mouse myotubes standing in for human skeletal muscle fibers.
    - divergence_type: BOUNDARY_OMISSION
      materiality: QUALIFYING
      description: >-
        The model contains no circulation, no transporters governing drug delivery, no
        immune system and no nervous system, so it cannot represent the exposure step
        above this node or the pain and autoimmune branches beside it.
    readouts:
    - name: Caspase-3/7 activity after simvastatin exposure
      target: Myocyte Bioenergetic Failure
      direction: INCREASED
      interpretation: >-
        Programmed cell death is engaged, and is suppressed by restoring the prenyl
        donor, which links the energy deficit to cell death within the same chain.
      evidence:
      - reference: PMID:21732624
        reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
        supports: SUPPORT
        evidence_source: IN_VITRO
        snippet: >-
          We also observed that GGPP suppressed the effects of simvastatin on
          caspase-3/7 and MTT activities, indicating a role for apoptosis as well
        explanation: >-
          The measurement behind this readout.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:21732624
    reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    quote_role: BACKGROUND
    snippet: >-
      We previously identified a cellular signature of statin-induced muscle toxicity in
      the C2C12 myotube.
    explanation: >-
      Establishes this line as the authors' established model of statin muscle toxicity,
      which is the basis for treating it as informative for the mechanism nodes it is
      linked to.
  notes: >-
    Recorded because three pathophysiology nodes and the edges between them rest on this
    one system. Without it the entry would state a molecular mechanism for human muscle
    injury without anywhere saying that a mouse myoblast line at a supratherapeutic
    concentration is what the claim is measured in.
animal_models:
- name: Zebrafish lovastatin myofiber toxicity model
  species: Zebrafish
  genotype: wild type
  description: >-
    Larval zebrafish exposed to lovastatin, with myofiber size and integrity read by
    myosin heavy chain staining. Used in a blinded design to test whether a suppressor
    identified in culture also protects muscle in a whole organism, which is the step
    the myotube system cannot take. The model's own validation is that geranylgeraniol
    supplementation suppresses the toxicity, the same prenyl-donor rescue seen in
    culture.
  publication: PMID:21732624
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Skeletal Muscle Fiber Necrosis
    relationship: PARTIALLY_RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: LOW
    model_scale: TISSUE
    description: >-
      Produces loss of myofiber size and integrity in an intact animal, which is the
      tissue-level outcome the culture model can only infer. It is the only system cited
      here in which statin exposure damages muscle within a whole organism.
    limitations: >-
      A teleost larva exposed to lovastatin in its water, scored on myofiber
      morphology. It shares no clinical endpoint with the human disease - no creatine
      kinase, no pain, no proximal distribution - and cannot speak to the autoimmune
      stratum, which requires an adaptive immune response to a human autoantigen.
    divergences:
    - divergence_type: SPECIES_MISMATCH
      materiality: QUALIFYING
      description: >-
        Zebrafish myofibers standing in for human skeletal muscle. The mevalonate
        pathway is conserved, and the rescue experiment is the evidence that the
        relevant biology is; the distribution and tempo of human disease are not
        modelled.
    - divergence_type: PROXY_QUANTITY
      materiality: QUALIFYING
      description: >-
        The measured quantity is myofiber size and striation on staining. The node's
        quantity is fiber necrosis releasing creatine kinase and myoglobin, which this
        model does not measure.
    readouts:
    - name: Myofiber size and integrity by myosin heavy chain staining
      target: Skeletal Muscle Fiber Necrosis
      direction: DECREASED
      interpretation: >-
        Loss of myofiber integrity in an intact animal after statin exposure.
      evidence:
      - reference: PMID:21732624
        reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
        supports: SUPPORT
        evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
        snippet: >-
          In a blinded study, we added either 1 or 5 µM lovastatin and 10 µM Gö6976 to
          zebrafish for 48 hours, and stained for myosin heavy chain to assess myofiber
          size and integrity.
        explanation: >-
          The design and the measurement behind this readout, including that the scoring
          was blinded.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:21732624
      reference_title: "A small-molecule screening strategy to identify suppressors of statin myopathy."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      quote_role: BACKGROUND
      snippet: >-
        The successful suppression of statin muscle toxicity in zebrafish by
        geranylgeraniol indicates that this model is a good one for testing our
        compounds of interest.
      explanation: >-
        The authors' stated basis for treating this model as informative: the
        prenyl-donor rescue that defines the mechanism in culture also works in the
        animal.
  notes: >-
    The passive-transfer mouse model named in the complement discussion's rationale is
    deliberately not recorded here. That work is cited in this entry only through the
    zilucoplan trial's framing of it, not from the primary report, so there is no source
    in this entry from which to state its genotype, readouts or limitations.
